# Open Threads / Pending Work

Older resolved/superseded threads are periodically moved to [OPEN_THREADS_ARCHIVE.md](OPEN_THREADS_ARCHIVE.md) (via `node scripts/archive_open_threads.mjs --apply`) to keep this file's per-session read cost down — nothing is deleted, just relocated. Still-pending items are backed by `OPEN_DECISION`/`RESEARCH_CLAIM` rows regardless, so archiving here never buries anything.
## ✅ 2026-08-19: Promotion pipeline — found and fixed a real false-promotion risk, deliberately stopped short of the full WPP fix

Investigating `promotion_pipeline_remaining_stuck_types`'s `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` half turned up something bigger than expected: its `SETUP_STATUS` row (`source=backtest_wpp_short_gap.mjs`, a bar-history *simulation*) says `ACTIVE`/N=30, while its real `active_setups` history clears 0 rows of the real-trade filter — and a 2026-08-10 fix means the generic real-forward scanner will **never** overwrite this row again (it's permanently skipped for this signal_name). The canonical eligibility functions (`isLiveEligible()`/`getCanonicalLiveStatus()`, `server/services/setupEligibility.js`) were trusting that `ACTIVE` label at face value — meaning this setup_type (and the same-shaped `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND`/`_BALANCE_FADE`) could fire live with zero real forward validation the moment they touched the right branch.

**Fixed** (commit `44b9a09`): added `BACKTEST_DERIVED_SETUP_STATUS_TYPES`, a small explicit list, and a real-forward N/EV check computed directly from `active_setups` (never from the untrustworthy `SETUP_STATUS` row) — wired into both `computeSuppressionSets()` (the main RTH candidate path) and `getCanonicalLiveStatus()` (any future poller). Verified live: `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` and `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` now correctly blocked despite their `ACTIVE` recommendation; an ordinary type (`IB_BEARISH`) unaffected. Server restarted, verified 200, no new errors.

**Deliberately stopped there**, per a direct user check-in mid-investigation ("is this worth it? I dont remember wpp") — the remaining half (rewiring the 2 hardcoded-`SHADOW` insert sites so WPP could actually promote once real N clears) requires untangling a shared branch several *other* unrelated setup_types also flow through, for a setup that's fired exactly once, real, ever. Not worth the risk for that payoff. Also not attempted: building `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_BALANCE_FADE`'s missing detector (a new live poller from scratch — bigger, separate scope). Resolves `OPEN_DECISION promotion_pipeline_remaining_stuck_types` (partially — the safety half is real and generalizes to any future type added to the list; the promotion half stays open but demoted to low-priority).

## ✅ 2026-08-19: Phase 0a time-windowed diagnostic built and run — 3/40 candidate cells, none trusted, one looks like a boundary artifact

Built `scripts/backtest_phase0a_time_windowed_diagnostic.mjs` from DeepSeek's fully-scoped 2026-08-03 plan (`scratch/deepseek_phase0_time_windowed_brainstorm.md`) — post-hoc conditions Phase 0's structural-breakout-retest population (0/8 gated cells passed, negative) by time-of-day window instead of pooling across the whole session, on the theory a real localized effect could be washed out by averaging. Reused Phase 0's real detection/simulation functions (`loadBars`/`detectPivots`/`scanRetests`/`simulateArm`, exported not reimplemented) and `getLandmark()` for Globex windows.

**Result**: 3 of 40 (regime, window, scale) cells show the conjunctive pattern (Arm1 beats Arm0 AND Arm2 AND Arm3, N≥20, rigor-clean) — `GLOBEX Pre_Globex k=10`, `RTH Opening_Spike k=20`, `RTH Mid_Morning k=30`. Per DeepSeek's own decision rule, none are trusted on their own — 40 cells screened, ~87% chance of at least one false positive by pure chance. Went a step further than the recorded plan required: cross-checked the two RTH candidates against the sliding 30-min/5-min-stride heatmap the same run already computed. **`Mid_Morning/k=30` looks real-ish** — a smooth, roughly monotonic climb across many overlapping bins (10:30–11:35 ET, ~$3.72 → ~$27.80). **`Opening_Spike/k=20` looks like a boundary artifact** — only the exact 9:30–10:00 bin is positive; every immediately-adjacent 5-minute-shifted bin reverses sharply negative (mostly −$5 to −$17 through 10:55–11:25). Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM phase0a_time_windowed_diagnostic` (PROVISIONAL). Full table + heatmap: `scratch/deepseek_phase0a_time_windowed_diagnostic.md`.

**2 real bugs found and fixed along the way**: both `backtest_overnight_pattern_discovery_permtest_20260720.mjs` and `backtest_structural_breakout_phase0.mjs` had an unconditional `main()`/`run()` call at module scope with no `import.meta.url` guard — the exact bug class already fixed once in this codebase (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`/`update_optimal_stops.mjs`). Exporting functions from either for reuse triggered its entire real execution as an import side effect: one wrote an extra (harmless, idempotent-upsert) `RESEARCH_CLAIM` row and clobbered `scratch/antigravity_response.md`; the other's `pool.end()` crashed the importing script's own persistence step mid-run — caught before any bad data was written (the crash happened at the very first query of the persist block, before any of its 44 rows updated). Both fixed with the same guard pattern, verified via a clean re-import before trusting the run.

Resolves `OPEN_DECISION phase0a_time_windowed_diagnostic_not_built`. **Not yet done, correctly deferred per Phase 0a's own decision rule**: Phase 0b (a dedicated window-native re-scan with chronological train/test split and `computeReplication()`) for `Mid_Morning/k=30` specifically — real, separate build work, only worth it because the diagnostic found something, not automatic. Explicitly do NOT build Phase 0b for `Opening_Spike/k=20` without first understanding why it doesn't survive a 5-minute boundary shift.

## 🔶 2026-08-19: Wider-target multiplier calibration resumed — 1.0x candidate + daily cadence built, persisted, DeepSeek review in flight

Resumed `OPEN_DECISION wider_target_calib_needs_deepseek_review`, paused earlier today in favor of the IB day-type work (now resolved). Implemented the two pending design refinements against `scripts/backtest_calibrated_wider_target.mjs`: added `1.0x` to `WIDER_MULTIPLIERS` (meaning "arm but bank T1 immediately, don't hold wider") and wired the script into `scripts/run_daily_calibration.sh` (daily, not weekly, per the already-recorded reasoning). Also added `recordClaim()` persistence — the script previously only wrote a `scratch/*.md` report, a no-dead-ends violation.

**The 1.0x addition paid off immediately on real data**: `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` and `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` both show negative EV delta at every multiplier ≥1.2x (armed trades lose more the wider they're held) — their leading candidate is now correctly 1.0x instead of the old grid forcing some wider hold with no way to express "don't." Pooled (N=277 real armed trades): leading candidate 2.5x (+$21.09/trade delta) but not rigor-clean anywhere except the `VALUE_FADE` bet_class (1.8x-2x, clean). Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM wider_target_multiplier_calibration` (PROVISIONAL — nothing here is wired live).

**Dispatched to DeepSeek for the code review this decision is named for** (`scratch/claude_request_deepseek.md`, explicit review-only header, running in background as of this note) — checking no-lookahead in the bar walk, direction-resolution correctness, whether the arming pre-check's fixed 1.5x dummy multiplier biases which trades become "eligible," baseline-simplification safety, bet_class-pooling sign-mixing risk, and `PNL_PER_POINT`/`COMMISSION` sourcing. **Next session/turn**: read `scratch/deepseek_response.md`, audit independently (not just trust), fix anything real, re-run, then decide whether to actually change the live `WIDER_TARGET_MULT=1.5` constant — that would be a separate, later decision; this pass is calibration only.

## ✅ 2026-08-19: `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` IB-break admission gate — tested (fresh session, per prior instruction), CONFIRMED negative

Direct continuation of the 2026-08-18 `promotion_pipeline_structural_fix_2026_08_16` thread's own explicit "next step," saved for a fresh session at the user's request. Built `scripts/pilot_momentum60_ib_break_gate.mjs`: re-derives the exact fire-once momentum-extreme event set `backtest_momentum60_daytype.mjs` uses, then for every event at/after 10:30 ET (matching the live poller's own `EVAL_START_ET_MIN`) computes IB break direction via the real, already-exported `computeIbBullBear()` (`server/services/caseEngine.js`) — never reimplemented — using only that day's own 9:30-10:30 ET bars, which are always fully elapsed by event time (no lookahead).

**Phase A (correlation)**: on the 6 known real TREND days, 32/51 fired events matched IB-break direction (62.7%) — looked promising in isolation. But the broader check (all 3,690 events across 2023-11-15–present) tells a different story: match rate is 47.4% on days that turn out TREND vs ~38-40% on BALANCE/TURBULENT days — a real but modest lift, not a decisive signal. Of the 1,505 events IB-break would ADMIT, only 24.7% actually land on a TREND day (precision) — the other 75% are false admits.

**Phase B (simulation, same-method baselines per CLAUDE.md's baseline-parity rule)**: using the live, currently-calibrated `OPTIMAL_STOP` row (stop=69pt/target=47.75pt, MNQ $2/pt) for every arm:
- Candidate (IB-break-match admitted): N=1505, WR=58.0%, **EV=-$2.55/trade**, `computeRigor().clean=false` (chronological thirds $0.89 / -$14.96 / $6.37, unstable).
- Unconditioned baseline (no gate, same stop/target): N=3690, EV=-$7.27/trade, rigor-clean.
- Rejected complement (same selection, IB-break didn't match — the confound-checklist control): N=2185, EV=-$10.51/trade, rigor-clean.
- Hindsight-TREND reference (not live-knowable, context only): N=783, EV=+$13.19/trade — this is the number the original `+$10.72` finding was chasing; confirms the underlying edge is real, just not accessible without the unknowable EOD label.

**Conclusion**: IB-break-match beats doing nothing but stays net-negative and unstable — wiring it as the live admission gate would convert the currently dead-but-harmless detector (0 live fires ever) into an active money-losing one, exactly the failure mode the prior session's own writeup warned against. **Left un-wired.** Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM momentum60_ib_break_admission_gate_test` (CONFIRMED negative), scheduled in `run_weekly_backtests.sh` (self-recalibrates as real bar history/day-type calibration grows — not a hard structural rejection). `OPEN_DECISION promotion_pipeline_structural_fix_2026_08_16` resolved with this result.

**Process note, corrected within the same session**: `flagDecision()` fully overwrites `decisionText` on re-flag of the same slug (confirmed by reading `flag_decision.mjs` — no append/merge), so this slug's original 2026-08-16 3-layer-fix content was fully replaced by the 2026-08-18 re-flag. Initially (wrongly) assumed Layer 1/3 were therefore still unbuilt and flagged a fresh `OPEN_DECISION` for them — then, before acting on it, re-verified directly against the code and found **Layer 1 (canonical `isLiveEligible()` gate) and Layer 3 for the 2 non-momentum60 pollers were already shipped 2026-08-16** (`server/routes/acd.js` imports `isLiveEligible`/`getCanonicalLiveStatus` from `server/services/setupEligibility.js`; `getOvernightLevelLiveStatus()`/`getStackVolBreakLiveStatus()` both call `getCanonicalLiveStatus()`) — already documented in the `shadowcandidates_hardcoded_no_promotion_path` and `time_expired_exclusion_pattern_broader_audit` decisions' own RESOLVED text, just not visible from this slug alone. Self-corrected, decision resolved as a no-op. The only genuinely open piece is `minuteBarSignalDetector.js`'s own Layer 3 migration (blocked on real forward data existing for that family, which is blocked on some admission gate passing — see the IB-break-gate negative result above). Nothing left to build here.

## ✅ 2026-08-19: `scripts/pilot_already_turned_entry.mjs` (Build 2 per-type study) — bug fixed, real negative result recorded

Root cause confirmed exactly as suspected: `t.fired_at` (a JS Date object — node-pg parses the naive
`timestamp without time zone` `fired_at` column by tagging its ET wall-clock digits as UTC) was
passed into a `$1::timestamptz` cast, and Postgres reinterpreted that value through the session's
`America/New_York` TimeZone setting, shifting every bar lookup by the ET/UTC offset (verified live:
a real 14:08 ET `fired_at` produced a bar lookup for 10:08, missing every match). Fix: fetch
`fired_at::text` (`fired_at_raw`) and cast with plain `::timestamp` (naive-to-naive, no timezone
reinterpretation) in both the median-range and closes queries — matches the CLAUDE.md convention on
naive timestamp parsing exactly.

Re-run confirms real classification: 601 real trades classified across all 10 tested setup_types
(previously 0). Genuine result, recorded via `recordClaim()` (slug-based upsert correctly overwrote
the earlier fabricated `sample_size=0, CONFIRMED` row — verified only one row now exists for this
slug): **0 of 10 setup_types show `DIRECTION_SURVIVES`** (favourable move beats adverse move at equal
distance, N≥20 both arms, rigor-clean) — `RESEARCH_CLAIM already_turned_entry_gate_per_type`,
`CONFIRMED` negative. Most types landed `INSUFFICIENT_DATA` (control or signal arm <20) rather than a
clean fail; `IB_BEARISH` was the closest near-miss (`DIRECTION_POSITIVE_NOT_RIGOR_CLEAN`, signal EV
+$22.80 vs control -$15.75, N=60/32, but `computeRigor().clean=false`); `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_SHORT`
was the one clean-rigor verdict and it went the other way (`GEOMETRY_ONLY_NO_REAL_SIGNAL`, control EV
$14.39 > signal EV $3.60). `OPEN_DECISION build2_already_turned_entry_gate_script_broken` resolved.

**Per DeepSeek's decisive recommendation, Build 2 is now settled (negative) — Build 1 (cross-setup-type
same-direction throttle) is next, still unstarted.** See its bar in the "four huge losses" entry below.

Day-type-conditioned IB stops (the first candidate fix) were tested via `scripts/backtest_ib_daytype_live_reassessment.mjs` and found NOT to help — real IB_BEARISH ~flat/-$1.32/trade once routed through the live `getLiveDayTypeRead()` reassessment engine rather than idealized hindsight; IB_BULLISH untestable (no calibration exists for its buckets). Two leads remained from the audit (Opus R3 "already-turned" entry-timing gate, Opus R4 cross-setup-type same-direction throttle) — DeepSeek ran a Phase-0 design critique on both before any code (`scratch/deepseek_cluster_loss_fixes_design_review.md`, full text preserved there).

**Shipped this session** (both defects DeepSeek flagged as cheap/independent/pre-study, `server/routes/acd.js`):
1. Removed the backwards cross-setup-type directional conflict gate (~line 8042, was `oppositeActive`/`DIRECTIONAL_CONFLICT_STAND_ASIDE`) — it only stood a candidate aside when an OPPOSITE-direction setup was already ACTIVE, confirmed near-inert (51 fires, 2 distinct days) and backwards relative to the real problem (SAME-direction stacking). Deliberately removed, not reversed — reversing would have silently shipped Build 1's unvalidated conclusion. See `OPEN_DECISION directional_conflict_gate_polarity_pending_build1`.
2. Re-added `IB_BULLISH: 30` to `REFIRE_COOLDOWN_MINUTES` (~line 8757) — its exclusion cited "globally SUPPRESSed today anyway," which went stale the moment IB_BULLISH moved under the temporary `CAPITAL_EXPOSURE_OVERRIDE` (`STOP_DAY_CLUSTERED`, added 2026-08-19). Without this, IB_BULLISH reverts to a zero-cooldown machine-gun path (13 real fires on 2026-08-06 alone) the instant that override clears. Same-family default (30min, matching IB_BEARISH), not independently validated for IB_BULLISH specifically.

**Build status — both settled negative, neither wired live:**
- **Build 2 (Opus R3, already-turned entry gate) — SETTLED NEGATIVE 2026-08-19.** Per-setup-type re-test (`scripts/pilot_already_turned_entry.mjs`) ran clean after a naive-timestamp bug fix (see the entry above this one) — 0 of 10 tested setup_types show `DIRECTION_SURVIVES`. `RESEARCH_CLAIM already_turned_entry_gate_per_type`, `CONFIRMED`. No live wiring, including SHADOW-informational.
- **Build 1 (Opus R4, cross-setup-type same-direction throttle) — SETTLED NEGATIVE 2026-08-19.** `scripts/pilot_same_direction_throttle.mjs` — dispatched to Gemini as a mine-and-run task (this codebase's standing "Gemini owns combinatorial/multi-scenario mining" rule), then audited before trusting: Gemini's first-pass baseline (n=325) didn't match Audit 8's own R4 baseline (n=344) for the same "ACTIVE" population — traced to a Claude-authored dispatch-spec error (wrongly restricted to `resolution IN ('STOP_HIT','TARGET_HIT')`; Audit 8's actual population is ACTIVE + any real `actual_pnl`, also including `TIME_EXPIRED`/`INVALIDATED` mark-to-market rows — verified directly, corrected population reproduces n=348/EV=$11.26 vs Audit 8's n=344/EV=$11.41). A second bug was caught while productionizing the reviewed script: a rewrite accidentally dropped the strict less-than check excluding tied `fired_at` timestamps from counting as "prior" fires of each other (real and common — several `setup_types` fire off the same 15s detection poll) — caught by comparing against the earlier verified run, fixed, re-confirmed reproducible. **Final, verified result**: DeepSeek's bar (A) requires ALL of (1) the 4+ rebound explained as a day-regime confound, (2) a genuine K/W plateau (immediate-neighbor same-sign, the `targetCalibrationService.js` plateau convention — not full-grid-row/column uniformity, which the original dispatch prompt over-specified), (3) `computeRigor().clean` on the primary K=2/W=30 kept arm. (1) and (2) both PASS — the 4+ bucket's edge is fully explained by 3 high-fire-count days ($724 of its $687.50 net P&L; the other 48 trades are flat/-$0.76 EV), and all 4 immediate K/W neighbors of K=2/W=30 are same-sign (+18.8% to +68.8%). (3) FAILS — the kept arm (n=196) is chronologically unstable (EV thirds $35.29 / $25.82 / -$3.76, declining into a negative final third). **This is a more precise negative than the original single-point estimate suggested**: not a mixture artifact, not an isolated spike — a real but currently-decaying pattern. `RESEARCH_CLAIM same_direction_throttle_stage1`, `CONFIRMED`. Scheduled in `run_weekly_backtests.sh` (still open, not a hard structural rejection — worth re-observing as real ACTIVE N grows). No live wiring, including SHADOW-informational. `OPEN_DECISION directional_conflict_gate_polarity_pending_build1` resolved — the removed directional-conflict gate stays removed, not reversed into a same-direction block, since there's no validated throttle to reverse it into.
- **Composition risk (naive AND-ing of both filters) is moot** — neither survived independently, so it never became a real question.

## ✅ 2026-08-19: `PD_POC_FADE_SHORT` "negative EV" concern (Opus Audit 8 incidental finding) — investigated, not a stat bug, surfaced a concrete case of the known RTH/Globex name-collision issue

User asked whether this had been addressed (it hadn't — flagged in Audit 8 as "clears the SUPPRESS gate on real_ev being above the -$5 floor while blended EV is well below" and "worst single contributor of the last 8 sessions"). Findings:
- The gate mechanism itself is correct/documented (SUPPRESS gates on `real_ev`, ACTIVE+SHADOW pooled and MTM-excluded, specifically to avoid the synthetic contamination `all_time_ev` suffers from — 50/136 rows are pre-2026-07-09 `UNKNOWN`-origin at -$24.52/trade, 64/136 are `BACKFILL`).
- SETUP_STATUS's snapshot (`real_ev=-2.40`) was already stale within the same day — live query at investigation time showed `real_ev=+7.42` (real_n 22→29) after 4 more real fires resolved. Normal weekly-cadence staleness, not a bug.
- The real finding: this is one of the 4 setup_types already named in the existing `pd_poc_fade_long_trail_lost_breakeven_trail_baseline` decision's notes as sharing an identical name between the RTH engine and `detectGlobexSetup()` (found 2026-08-11, never quantified until now). Session-bucketing the real ACTIVE (capital-risk) fires: **RTH-session n=7, EV=-$38.13 — but none of those 7 are genuine price resolutions** (5 are the already-investigated 2026-07-22 structural-invalidation cluster at a nominal -$1 each, 2 are today's still-open positions force-closed at end-of-day mark). **All 13 real, cleanly-resolved (TARGET_HIT/STOP_HIT) ACTIVE trades are Globex-session**, EV=+$2.42. The RTH engine's live PD_POC_FADE_SHORT has never actually produced a clean resolution, good or bad — Opus's "worst contributor" read was really "Globex is working, RTH hasn't resolved yet," hidden behind one shared setup_type name.
- No action taken (no evidence to suppress, no evidence to endorse RTH firing either — genuinely unresolved). This is a concrete, load-bearing case for the still-unstarted `globex_ambiguous_names_need_session_backfill` item (session-tag `active_setups`/`getBetClass()` so RTH and Globex fires of the same 4 shared-name types score separately) — not yet built, asked user whether to prioritize it next.

## 🔶 2026-08-18: Exit-timing mechanisms need a holistic reassessment — 4 independently-built mechanisms, never looked at together

User request, triggered by walking through a real trade (id 99859) where `bar6_exit_recommended` fired correctly per its own validated logic (recovered early + only ~13.6% progress toward target by bar 6, the exact profile the frozen `targetDistFraction<0.873` rule found underperforms on average) but the trade went on to run 112pt MFE before resolving 31 bars later — not a bug, just a validated-but-probabilistic signal looking wrong in hindsight on an outlier. Prompted the broader observation: this system now has 4 separate, independently-validated exit-timing mechanisms (bar-6 checkpoint, the wider-target runner, tonight's slow+deep early-exit research, breakeven-then-trail) that have never been reviewed as a family — do any ever apply to the same trade simultaneously, is there a coherent precedence rule, is the bar-6 cutoff still calibrated on current data, should the slow+deep research eventually share machinery with bar-6 rather than becoming a 5th independent mechanism. Not urgent (all 4 are informational-only or SHADOW-only, zero real capital at risk from any individually) — recorded as `OPEN_DECISION exit_logic_family_holistic_reassessment_20260818` (MEDIUM) rather than left as conversation-only, per explicit user request. Full scope and the 4 mechanisms' current states are in the decision text.

## 🔶 2026-08-18: `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` gate — root cause confirmed by DeepSeek, proposed fix WRONG, real research task identified (IB-break direction), saved for a fresh session

Continuation of the entry below, same day. Dispatched DeepSeek to QA the day_type-gate diagnosis before writing any code, per explicit user instruction ("ask deepseek to qa the issue before you start coding it... there might be other issues"). Result:

**Diagnosis independently confirmed** — DeepSeek verified the 20:20 ET write timing directly (crontab, calibration logs), confirmed no other intraday writer of `acd_daily_log.day_type` exists anywhere in the codebase, and independently re-derived the 6-day empirical check (matching numbers). Also found the root cause of the *original mistake*: both the detector's own header comment and its originating backtest script's header both falsely assert day_type reclassifies live at IB close (10:30 ET) — it doesn't; only the *label* is computed from EOD data, and only *written* at 8:20 PM.

**But the proposed fix (swap in `dayTypeReassessmentService.js`'s live estimate) would NOT have worked** — a real, load-bearing catch. The momentum-extreme crossing happens at 10:30-10:32 ET on all 6 real TREND days checked, which is the exact instant that estimator is structurally incapable of reading TREND yet (its first reassessment checkpoint requires being past IB close, and it's "0% detectable at 10:00 ET, 36.5% by 11:30" per its own documentation). Deeper problem: this setup's entire +$10.72/trade edge is conditioned on the EOD label specifically — the unconditioned version (fire every day) is an *already-tested loser* (-$15.75/trade). Neither "drop the gate" nor "make it informational-only" is safe; both silently convert a dead-but-harmless detector into an active money-losing one.

**User-confirmed insight, same session**: 10:30 ET is also exactly when Initial Balance closes — meaning IB break direction becomes knowable at the *same instant* the momentum crossing happens, zero lag, unlike the day-type estimator. This matches DeepSeek's own independently-suggested candidate: gate the continuation trade on "IB already broke in the same direction as the extreme" instead of the unknowable EOD label. **This is the real next step** — explicitly saved for a fresh session (user instruction: do not start building tonight). Full test spec recorded in the `OPEN_DECISION`.

**One correction made to DeepSeek's own review before trusting it**: it flagged a related-but-different gate (`isTrendCounterFade()`) as "the most dangerous" live risk from this same bug family. Checked against this project's own decision history and found this is a re-discovery of something already resolved 2026-08-03 — that exact gate was already backtested with the live reassessment engine and found net-harmful (-$3,238.60), so it's *deliberately* left inert, not a live risk. Not passing that framing forward; the genuinely open sibling item (`dtclass_other_3_gates_untested`) is unaffected, just freshly reconfirmed with updated line numbers.

**Two minor, low-priority bugs also found** (independent of the day_type gate, currently masked by it): no market-hours/holiday guard in the detector (fixing the gate without adding this would expose stale-bar-data reads on holidays), and a rising-edge latch that's skipped on early returns (harmless today, would misbehave once any gate here becomes passable). Fix alongside any real admission-condition change, not separately. Also confirmed: `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_BALANCE_FADE`'s absence is a deliberate, already-backtested negative finding, not an accidental gap — no action needed there.

## 🔶 2026-08-18: `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` structurally can never fire live — root cause found, fix needs its own validation, not a quick swap

Following up on the `promotion_pipeline_structural_fix_2026_08_16` decision's own stated "next step" (verify `test_invariants.mjs` check [20]'s WARN list shrinks as real fires accumulate) — it won't, ever, under the current code, and this is now proven rather than suspected. `minuteBarSignalDetector.js`'s `detectMomentum60Trend()` (the only insert path for this setup_type) gates on `acd_daily_log.day_type = 'TREND'` during its 10:30am-3pm ET eval window, but `acd.js`'s own comment confirms that column isn't written until the 20:20 ET nightly `derive_day_types.js` run — 5+ hours after the eval window closes. The gate is NULL, always, during the only window that matters, so it can structurally never pass.

**Verified empirically, not just by reading the code**: reconstructed the exact same momentum-vs-rolling-threshold logic against real bar data for all 6 real TREND days in the last 35 — every single one shows momentum blowing far past the threshold (200-400pt day swings vs a ~90-100pt threshold), so the underlying momentum condition is not rare at all; the day_type gate alone is what's blocking every fire. Confirmed via direct query: zero rows exist for this setup_type, any `origin_status`, ever.

**Not a quick fix**: a live, no-lookahead day-type estimator does exist (`dayTypeReassessmentService.js`) but was explicitly tested and rejected as a live gate input on 2026-08-03 for a *different* reason — 70.6% false-positive rate specifically at the moment a level-fade touches (a fade-touch structurally looks like a trend in progress). That confound doesn't obviously apply to a raw momentum-extreme trigger (arguably a much more natural fit for an engine built to catch "fresh range expansion"), but swapping it in without testing it against THIS specific trigger condition would repeat the same mistake in a different color. Recorded as the concrete next step in `OPEN_DECISION promotion_pipeline_structural_fix_2026_08_16` (updated, still HIGH/PENDING): backtest whether the live estimator, evaluated at the moment of each historical momentum-extreme crossing, would have correctly read TREND — before touching the live gate.

## ✅ 2026-08-18: `update_optimal_stops.mjs` circuit-breaker/thin-N bug hunt — 2 real bugs found (DeepSeek, 2 dispatches) and fixed live, one live-capital exposure question left genuinely open

User asked DeepSeek to critique the live stop-calibration system as a whole (`scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`), starting from the already-open `optimal_stop_circuit_breaker_retripped_20260812` decision (3 setup types frozen since 2026-08-16). DeepSeek's first pass (architecture critique) found the framing of that decision was itself wrong: **`GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG`'s "fresh sweep" attempt (stop=68) was never an honest sweep at all.** Both `sweepOptimalStopAndTarget()`/`sweepOptimalStopAndTargetChronological()` gate each MAE-percentile candidate individually (`requiredN = ceil(MIN_N/(1-pct))`), and candidates start at p25, so a type needs real N>=27 (not >=20) before ANY candidate is selectable — at N=25, both sweeps silently return null and the code fell through to a *different*, self-referential fallback (p75 of the CENSORED `mae_points` column — precisely the censoring bug `uncensoredMaeCandidates()` was built to escape) while still labeling the row as if it had swept. Independently verified directly (not taken on faith): confirmed the exact requiredN math (27), confirmed the live stored `notes.method='p75mae-real-fallback'` for every recent GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG run, and confirmed via direct query that its real trade population (N=25) is 64% concentrated in its top 5 trading days.

**Second, related bug DeepSeek found in the same pass**: `applyCircuitBreaker()` compares an attempted value against the prior stored value with no idea whether that prior was ever itself a validated calibration — so it can (and was) defending an admitted placeholder indefinitely, refusing every subsequent real calibration attempt from ever replacing it.

**Fixed, DeepSeek-code-reviewed (2nd dispatch, "safe to run" verdict with one narrow pre-existing edge case flagged, not introduced by this diff), dry-run-verified against the live DB, then RUN FOR REAL**:
1. New `MIN_SWEEPABLE_N` (=27, derived from the same formula the sweep functions already use, not a second hardcoded number) — types below it now route to the honest `volatility-scaled-default` placeholder instead of the censored fallback.
2. `applyCircuitBreaker()` now bypasses the pct-change gate when the PRIOR value was itself a placeholder (`volatility-scaled-default`/`p75mae-real-fallback`) AND the attempted value is genuinely swept — verified safe for legacy rows with no stored `method` (`isPlaceholderMethod(null) === false`, falls through to the normal gate).
3. Added an informational `computeRigor` day-clustering diagnostic to every genuinely-swept row's notes (never gates anything, matching this codebase's standing convention for `computeRigor`).

**Live result** (verified directly against `performance_audit`, not just console output): `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG` stop moved 32→37 — small, conservative, and now an honest placeholder instead of a fake-precision 68. `IB_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` (a second, independent type hitting the same bug class) got unstuck for real: 29/30 (fake) → 25/50 (genuine `chronological-sweep-real`), EV improved +$16.74. `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_SHORT`/`IB_BULLISH` remain correctly frozen exactly as before (their priors were real, not placeholders — the bypass correctly doesn't apply to them). `test_invariants.mjs` re-run clean (7 FAIL — down from a prior 8, all pre-existing/expected: 5 unrelated `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` gaps + the 2 still-correctly-tripped breaker FAILs; 30 WARN baseline), eslint clean.

**New finding the fix itself surfaced** (from the new day-clustering diagnostic): `IB_BULLISH`'s "genuine" swept calibration is 97.1% concentrated in its top-5 days (only 7 distinct real trading days total behind it), and `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_SHORT` is 76.3% clustered — both of the STILL-frozen types are themselves thin/clustered even in their attempted values, not just `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG`'s case.

**Genuinely still open, not resolved, not silently executed**: whether `GLOBEX_VWAP_FADE_LONG` (N=25, still below `MIN_SWEEPABLE_N`) and/or `IB_BULLISH` (97.1% day-clustered even when swept) should be demoted to SHADOW or sized down given real capital is currently exposed on either an honest placeholder or an extremely thin/clustered real sample. Both DeepSeek dispatches independently recommended managing this via exposure, not by picking between two similarly-unvalidated numbers — but this codebase's existing suppression pipeline (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`→`SETUP_STATUS`) is WR/EV-driven, not stop-validity-driven, so there's no existing automatic mechanism for this and it needs a deliberate human call. Recorded in the `OPEN_DECISION` (updated, still `PENDING`, elevated context).

**Process note**: two DeepSeek dispatches this session hit the same "stall past its own timeout with zero output growth" pattern narrower re-scoping had fixed earlier — the 2nd (code-review) dispatch this time completed fine (~11min), but its response file grew a large trailing block of NUL bytes + the invocation harness's own self-referential transcript AFTER the actual review content (lines 1-245 were the real review; everything after was `cline`'s own tooling logging its live session into the same output file it was asked to write to). Not a corruption of the actual answer — the real content was intact and internally consistent with its own closing summary — but worth knowing: if a DeepSeek response file is unexpectedly huge, check for a clean early section and a matching closing summary rather than assuming the whole file is meaningful content.

## 🔶 2026-08-18: "slow+deep adverse-grind early exit" — Phase-0 plan steps 1-4 BUILT this session, DeepSeek code-review in flight, still NOT wired live

Continuation of the entry below (same day, post-`/clear`, per the prior session's own explicit instruction to build the plan in a fresh session). Built `scripts/backtest_slow_deep_early_exit.mjs` — consolidates the trusted pilot Part 4 (market-exit-at-confirmation) into a real, re-checkable script implementing DeepSeek's steps 1-3: a finer 0.40-0.85 (+0.90 diagnostic) depth-trigger sweep reusing the pilot's exact mechanics, a `bet_class` split of the 75%-trigger SLOW bucket via `getBetClass()`, and `computeRigor`/`computeReplication` on the result. Step 4 (cheap, parallel): added `topBetClass`/`topBetClassShare`/`by_bet_class` to `audit_wider_target_live.mjs`'s existing monitor notes. Step 5 stays deferred (already gated by the claim's `unblockCondition`). Both scripts run for real against the live DB (not just eyeballed) and their writes read back and confirmed in the same turn, per standing convention.

**Real result, not yet confirmable as pooled**: the finer sweep reproduces the exact headline number from the pilot (n=327, delta=$2.32 at 75%/SLOW — confirms the port is faithful) and shows a mostly-positive shape across trusted (n≥20) cells (7/10). But the bet_class split found `CONTINUATION_LEGACY` (n=97) has a **negative** delta (-1.45) — opposite sign from the pooled result — while `VALUE_FADE` (n=177, +2.42) and `GLOBEX_LEVEL` (n=47, +9.12) agree with pooled. `computeRigor` came back **not clean** (chronological thirds: $0.08 → $9.51 → -$2.62, unstable). This is exactly the "pooling would be actively wrong for some families" scenario the prior session's spec doc warned about — the script correctly held the verdict at `PROVISIONAL` rather than auto-confirming, and `recordClaim()` was upserted with an `unblockCondition` naming the one remaining thin bet_class (`FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL`, n=6). Wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh` so it self-recalibrates weekly as real N grows, per standing convention — no manual re-invocation needed.

**DeepSeek code-review dispatched, partially completed, killed after ~30min stuck past its own 15-min timeout with zero file growth for 27min** — but not before finding 2 real bugs, both independently verified against the actual code (not taken on faith) and fixed same session:
1. **The trades query had no `ORDER BY`**, but `computeRigor()`'s chronological "thirds" stability check assumes chronological input order (confirmed by checking other callers, e.g. `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s `ORDER BY setup_type, fired_at ASC`). Added `ORDER BY fired_at ASC`. This materially changed the thirds output (`ev1/ev2/ev3` went from `0.08/9.51/-2.62` with `boundaryStraddle=true` to `-2.88/7.46/2.39` with `boundaryStraddle=false`) — same overall PROVISIONAL/not-clean conclusion, but now for the right reason.
2. **`unblockCondition.setupTypes` was filled with `bet_class` labels** (e.g. `'FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL'`) instead of real `setup_type` names — `test_invariants.mjs` check `[11]` queries `active_setups WHERE setup_type = ANY($1)`, so no row would ever match a bet_class string and the unblock condition could never fire even as real data grows. Fixed to collect the real `setup_type` values actually observed in the thin bet_class's slice (`FAILED_SWEEP_REVERSAL_LONG`/`_SHORT`). Verified check `[11]` runs clean against the corrected claim.

**Follow-up dispatch (narrower scope, 8min timeout — completed cleanly this time, ~5.5min, no stall)** answered the 2 remaining interpretive questions DeepSeek didn't reach before the first dispatch was killed:
1. `computeReplication`'s `replicates=true` (1 held-out bet_class, n=6) was **vacuous, not meaningful** — with `heldOutN=1` every term degenerates to "is this one thin unit favorable," re-admitting through the back door the exact n<20 sample the trusted-only gate was built to exclude. The function's real use case (`docs/CONVENTIONS_DETAIL.md` confound-checklist item 4) is top-K-selected-from-a-wide-sweep (e.g. 6-of-48 setup_types); this finding was never that shape. **Dropped `computeReplication` from the script entirely.**
2. The pooled PROVISIONAL number was **rescoped to family-gated**, per the spec doc's own pre-registered step-2 fallback ("if only some families clear -> rescope to a family-gated rule, never ship pooled"). The recorded claim now leads with VALUE_FADE + GLOBEX_LEVEL only (n=224, **$3.83/trade** — arithmetic independently re-verified: 177×2.42+47×9.12=856.98, /224=3.83), explicitly excludes CONTINUATION_LEGACY by name (n=97, -$1.45/trade, opposite sign, real N not thin data), and keeps the full pooled figure ($2.32/trade, n=327) in `extra` as context only, not the headline. Re-ran `computeRigor` on the family-gated population specifically (not the old full-pooled one) — still not clean (chronological thirds fade from +$9.47 to -$3.04), so the claim correctly stays PROVISIONAL, now for a real family-gated-specific reason rather than carrying over the old pooled-population instability.

Both changes implemented in `scripts/backtest_slow_deep_early_exit.mjs`, re-run against the live DB, write confirmed, `test_invariants.mjs` check `[11]` clean, eslint clean. **Process note for future DeepSeek dispatches**: the first (full-review) dispatch stalled 30+min past its 15min timeout with zero output growth; a second, narrowly-scoped 2-question follow-up with an 8min timeout completed cleanly in 5.5min. Inconclusive whether scope size was the actual cause (the stall pattern — alive process, zero file growth — looked more like a hung tool call than gradual multi-step slowness) but narrower dispatches are cheap to retry and worth defaulting to when a broad review stalls.

**Still not wired live** — nothing in this build touches acd.js or any insert path. The only artifacts are a new scheduled backtest script and 3 new fields in an existing monitor's notes.

## ✅ 2026-08-18: zero live ACTIVE fires all day traced to 5 duplicate server supervisors — single-instance takeover consolidated into one shared lib

User reported over 40 setups had fired today but none went live (`origin_status='ACTIVE'`), versus a 4-13/day baseline every prior day this week. Traced to two compounding, now-fixed problems, not a suppression-logic bug in `acd.js` itself (individually checked `SUPPRESSED_FADE`/`CLUSTER_ALREADY_FIRED`/etc. — all functioning as designed):

1. **5 simultaneous `nodemon server/index.js` supervisors** were alive at once: one from a stale post-WSL-reboot `restart.sh` session (~1:58 AM) plus a full separate `npm start` tree from the same reboot event still serving a 12-hour-old frontend on port 3000, and three more from separate sessions independently running bare `npm run server` across the day (8:55 AM, 12:59 PM, 1:08 PM) — none of which had any cleanup/takeover logic. All were fighting over port 3002 with each other and with `trading-journal-server.service`, producing a wall of `UNCAUGHT_EXCEPTION`/`EADDRINUSE` restarts concentrated 11:30 AM–1:09 PM ET (prime RTH) — a real detection-availability gap during that window, likely why the genuinely-`ACTIVE`-rated setup types (`GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG/SHORT`, `MOMENTUM_60m_*`, `IB_BEARISH/BULLISH`) never touched at all today.
2. `trading-journal-server.service` tripped its 2026-08-09 failure-burst override and went permanently `failed` (systemd does not auto-recover from this state) — production was being served only by one of the ad hoc `npm run server` processes, with nothing left to restart it if that process died.

**Root cause, not just symptom**: `restart.sh` had silently duplicated `start.sh`'s `cleanup_ports()` by hand and drifted out of sync — missing the "stop systemd first" step (`docs/OPEN_THREADS.md`'s own 2026-08-17 entry below flagged this exact drift as "worth a look next session" and it wasn't picked up until it caused a real outage) and missing the EXIT-trap/health-monitor handback entirely. Bare `npm run server` (CLAUDE.md's own documented "just the backend" shortcut) had *none* of this protection at all.

**Fixed**: extracted the shared logic into `scripts/lib/server-lifecycle.sh` (`cleanup_ports`/`stop_systemd_server`/`handback_on_exit`/`start_health_monitor`) — `start.sh` now sources it, `restart.sh` is now a thin wrapper that delegates to `start.sh` entirely (removes the duplicate-script drift risk for good rather than re-fixing it a second time), `stop.sh` sources it too, and `npm run server` now runs `scripts/dev-server-guard.sh` (same takeover/cleanup/handback, skipped when invoked as a nested child of `start.sh` itself via a `SERVER_GUARD_PARENT_MANAGED` env check, so the two layers don't fight). Added a loud backstop to `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` (`DUPLICATE SERVER SUPERVISORS` check) that fires if >1 `nodemon server/index.js` is ever found running again, in case something still bypasses all three entry points. Cleaned up all 5 stale processes live, `systemctl --user reset-failed` on the service (left `inactive`, not restarted, since the current dev-mode process — started cleanly at 1:11 PM after the fix commit — still holds port 3002; systemd will take over next time the port is properly handed back via `./stop.sh` or a `start.sh` session ending). Verified: all 4 shell scripts pass `bash -n`, `session-start.sh` runs clean end-to-end with the new check producing no false positive against the now-single live supervisor.

## ✅ 2026-08-18: wider-target mechanism full bug hunt (DeepSeek) — B1/B2/C1 + a separately-found 4th SHADOW insert site, all fixed, DeepSeek-reviewed clean

**Resolved 2026-08-18 (post-clear continuation)**: the fix scope below (B1/B2/C1, DeepSeek-QA-approved in a prior session) was implemented, plus the separately-found 4th insert site (see the OR5 entry just below — `wider_target_mult` was entirely missing from the "suppressed near-level audit" branch's INSERT). DeepSeek's code-review of the full diff came back clean: no correctness bugs, implementation matches every QA correction exactly (confirmed `resolveDirection(row)` not bare `inferDirection`, confirmed the B2/extendTarget `!isSessionEnd` guards land on the right branches, confirmed C1's predicate inversion, and did a full manual placeholder recount on the 4th site's INSERT — no off-by-one). Two stale comments it flagged were fixed in the same commit; a `BANKED_SESSION_END` method-string split was flagged as optional/not required and left as-is (zero downstream consumers of the string either way). Live-confirmed on the running server: a real row (id 99540, `IB_MID_SCALP_FADE_LONG`) inserted through the previously-broken 4th site with `wider_target_mult` set, which was structurally impossible before this fix. Verified: 19/19 synthetic walker tests (up from 8 tests/pre-fix), eslint clean, `test_invariants.mjs` unchanged from the pre-existing 8 FAILURE/30 WARNING baseline, server restarted and healthy throughout. Committed as `15a0502`.

**Immediate follow-up, same day, user-caught**: the code fix is forward-only — it changes what future INSERTs write, it cannot retroactively tag rows already sitting in the table. User directly identified 8 real SHADOW trades that fired through the broken 4th site *before* the fix deployed (13:10:20 ET) and reached T1 within `MAX_BARS_TO_T1_FOR_WIDER` bars, meaning they should have gotten the mechanism's chance to widen but banked at plain T1 instead — a real "no dead ends" gap, not a hypothetical one. Built `scripts/backfill_wider_target_4th_site_miss_20260818.mjs` (dry-run first, then `--apply`) reusing the exact same `stepWiderTarget()`/`resolveDirection()` the live mechanism and the existing per-trade wider-target counterfactual endpoint both use — re-walked each of the 8 rows' real price bars, all 8 reconstructed cleanly (0 skipped/mismatched via the endpoint's own consistency check), all resolved `WIDER_TARGET_HIT` with +$37 to +$57 deltas vs. their old plain-T1 outcome. Backed up first (`active_setups_widertarget_4thsite_backfill_backup_20260818`, catalogued in `docs/DB_BACKUP_CATALOG.md`) and self-verified after write. `audit_wider_target_live.mjs`'s live N went from 2 armed → 10 armed as a direct result. `test_invariants.mjs` unchanged from baseline post-backfill. This population is one-time and closed (the code bug that produced it no longer exists going forward) — no further recheck needed for this specific gap, though the general lesson (a fixed INSERT site never retroactively repairs pre-fix rows) is worth remembering for any future insert-site bug of this shape.

**Original scope (below, for history) — now fully implemented:**

Direct follow-up to the `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG_GAP_DOWN` build below: while investigating why only 1 of 9 fast winners on 2026-08-18 got the wider-target mechanism armed, found the mechanism has a real, unwired 4th SHADOW insert path (the "suppressed near-level audit" branch in `acd.js` ~line 7309-7321 never included `wider_target_mult` in its column list — the majority source of SHADOW rows system-wide, since most setup_types are suppressed by default). User then asked for a full DeepSeek adversarial bug hunt across the entire mechanism (`widerTargetWalker.js`, all insert sites, `resolveSetupsByPrice()`'s consumption, the counterfactual endpoint, the status endpoint, `audit_wider_target_live.mjs`, the synthetic test, the frozen backtest). Full DeepSeek report is in this session's transcript / `scratch/deepseek_response.md` as of the dispatch below — summarized here so nothing is lost across the context clear that prompted this entry.

**Confirmed bugs found:**
- **B1 (HIGH)**: `scripts/audit_wider_target_live.mjs`'s direction-exclusion check (lines ~48-50, ~91-102) compares against a hardcoded copy of the OLD, now-fixed buggy `isLongSetup()` heuristic — but `resolveSetupsByPrice()` was already fixed elsewhere to use the correct `resolveDirection()`/`inferDirection()`. This means the audit script now WRONGLY EXCLUDES correctly-resolved `_GAP_*` variant armed rows (e.g. `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP`, the new `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG_GAP_DOWN`) from its own `armedN`/`meanDelta`/verdict — silently undercounting and biasing the exact number this mechanism's promotion decision depends on. The script's OWN independent reconstruction check (lines ~108-114, comparing stored `actual_pnl` against a fresh recompute from `price_at_resolution`) already catches genuine corruption on its own and makes the stale direction check redundant even when it used to be correct.
- **B2 (LOW)**: `server/services/widerTargetWalker.js` (`stepWiderTarget()`, lines ~48-61) has a same-bar contradiction: if a trade's T1 is hit on/after the 16:00 RTH-close bar AND within the fast-arming window, the function simultaneously arms (`newState = {widening:true}`) AND resolves `TIME_EXPIRED`/`MARK_TO_MARKET` in the same return — a real contract violation (docs say it returns `{state, resolution}`, not both-at-once contradictory state). Practical effect: such a trade gets mislabeled `MARK_TO_MARKET` instead of `WIDER_TIME_EXPIRED`, so `audit_wider_target_live.mjs` never counts it as armed even though it qualified. Narrow window (must fire minutes before 4pm and touch T1 at/after 16:00) but a real logic wart, uncovered by the synthetic test suite.

**Latent risk (not yet biting, cheap to fix now):**
- **C1**: the pattern-setup/`shadowCandidates` insert site (`acd.js` ~line 8853-8859) checks `ABSORPTION_LONG`/`COIL_SURGE*` exclusions but NOT `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS[type]?.trailSignalName`, unlike the other 2 wired sites (~8327, ~8714). Harmless today only because no `_TRAIL` variant currently flows through `shadowCandidates`'s roster — but if one ever gets added there, it would get flagged `wider_target_mult` with no `runner_trail_width`, silently using the wrong exit mechanism.

**Confirmed fine (don't re-litigate)**: full audit of all 7 real `INSERT INTO active_setups` sites confirms the 3 exit-mechanism flags (`wider_target_mult`/`runner_trail_width`/`extend_target_level`) are never set together on any row; direction signs correct everywhere; the earlier NUMERIC-as-string bug class fully closed; bar-counting convention consistent; a naming collision (`widerTarget` used for 3 unrelated things across `detectGlobexSetup()`'s Globex candidates vs. the mechanism's own local vs. the counterfactual endpoint) confirmed to have zero functional cross-contamination (different function scopes) but flagged as a maintainability hazard worth a rename someday. Also noted, lower priority: a dashboard `nActiveCurrent` count undercounts true real-ACTIVE N (informational only), 2 frozen-backtest methodology inconsistencies (window-length divergence, mixed P&L conventions — don't affect anything live), and `MIN_N=20` duplicated in 2 files (already documented). SHORT-direction synthetic test coverage is thin (1 test vs. 6 for LONG) — a SHORT sign regression could currently pass the suite.

**Fix scope (written, DeepSeek QA dispatched same session, NOT yet built)**:
1. **B1 fix**: delete `isLongSetupBuggy()` and the `correctDirection !== buggyDirection` exclusion branch in `audit_wider_target_live.mjs`; keep only the `inferDirection()===null` exclusion and the reconstruction-mismatch exclusion (already a strictly stronger, still-valid check). Update the stale header comments describing the removed `isLongSetup()` helper.
2. **B2 fix**: in `widerTargetWalker.js`'s pre-arm branch, don't arm when `isSessionEnd` is also true on the same bar — fall through to the plain bank-at-t1 outcome instead (there's no remaining session time to benefit from arming anyway), keeping `{state, resolution}` mutually exclusive per the function's documented contract.
3. **C1 fix**: add the same `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS[shadow.type]?.trailSignalName != null` exclusion to the `shadowCandidates` insert site's `wider_target_mult` condition, aligning all 3 wired sites' exclusion predicates (defensive, currently a no-op).
4. Consider (lower priority, ask user before doing): renaming `detectGlobexSetup()`'s `widerTarget`/`widerStop`/`widerWindowNew` candidate props (e.g. to `overnightWindowTarget`/`overnightWindowStop`) to remove the naming collision; adding SHORT-direction coverage to the synthetic test suite for the untested scenarios (stop-after-arm, slow-T1-bank, same-bar T1+stop, stop-before-arm, session-end).

**DeepSeek QA on the fix scope came back (same session, before context clear) — approved with corrections. Implement per this, not the raw scope above:**
- **B1**: proceed, but swap `inferDirection(setup_type)` for `resolveDirection({setup_type, stop_level, t1_level})` in the audit script's loop (one-line change, the fields are already SELECTed) rather than keeping bare `inferDirection` — QA caught that the plan's "the null exclusion is a genuine unresolvable-direction case" claim is false: `inferDirection` is name-only and returns null for `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` (present in the shadowCandidates roster), which the live path actually resolves via `resolveDirection`'s price-fallback (`t1 > stop`). Also: **add a synthetic test** for this exact case (`t1Hit && barCount<=max && isSessionEnd` → `TARGET_HIT`/`PRICE_CLEAN`, `widening:false`) — none of the current 8 tests cover it.
- **C1**: proceed exactly as scoped.

Decide whether to fold this into the same commit as B2 or explicitly defer it with its own note — don't let it get silently dropped, it's the same bug class in a sibling branch.
- **On the expected `armedN` jump post-B1-fix**: still real, but QA refined it — only rows that pass the reconstruction check land in `armedN`; any row that's genuinely corrupted (not just wrongly excluded) will move from the `direction_mismatch` exclusion reason to the `actual_pnl_mismatch` exclusion reason instead, not into `armedN`. **Annotate the verdict-transition `flagDecision`, don't suppress it** — the corrected number is more accurate than the old one, so hiding the transition would hide a legitimate signal. If it fires, resolve/annotate it noting the transition is a counting-bug correction, not a real performance change. Optional cheap addition: break `n_excluded` down by reason in `notes` so this is self-documenting for whoever reads it later.

## ✅ 2026-08-18: `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG_GAP_DOWN` conditional variant built — a data-driven salvage of a suppressed OR fade, SHADOW-only, monitor at real N=50

User noticed `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG`/`OR5_MID_FADE_LONG` (5-min opening-range low/mid fades) were suppressed despite decent-looking blended stats, and asked whether a fire-time-knowable market condition could "rescue" either one — the same shape as this codebase's one proven conditional-variant win, `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP`. Full arc, DeepSeek involved at every stage (design critique before mining, methodology audit after a first Gemini mining attempt had 4 real bugs, a second methodology audit after the corrected re-run, and a promotion-decision review before any code was written):

**Mining (Gemini, 2 attempts)**: first attempt tested 6 candidate bar-state conditions (gap-into-level, first-vs-repeat touch, before/after IB close, approach velocity, normalized distance from open, opening-bar volume) against the OR family's real bar-history population, but DeepSeek's audit found 4 confirmed bugs — no touch-cooldown (5.9-9x overcounted N), a flat uncalibrated 40/30 stop-target applied to every setup regardless of its own calibration, wrong commission ($1 vs the corrected $2 round-trip), and cross-day lookahead (the backtest walk could resolve a late-day touch against tomorrow's bars, a real no-lookahead violation). Second attempt (Gemini's 2-strikes-then-Claude-takes-over budget) fixed all 4; corrected result: `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG`'s gap-into-level condition (session gapped DOWN into the level) cleared every bar — real touch-count now matches the officially tracked backfill population (341 vs ~330-336), `computeRigor` CLEAN, `computeReplication` PASS (caveated: only checked against other OR-family cells). `OR5_MID_FADE_LONG`'s equivalent finding FAILED rigor despite a bigger raw EV swing — correctly not pursued, a real instance of the stability check catching a tempting-looking but non-robust result.

**Promotion-decision review (DeepSeek)**: Claude's instinct was to force the new variant SHADOW-only (`recommendation='THIN_N'`) rather than let it compute `ACTIVE` from the backtest alone, reasoning it should diverge from the `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` precedent since that one came from a single post-mortem observation, not a corrected sweep survivor. **DeepSeek's DB query found the actual premise was wrong**: `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` has fired exactly ONCE ever (SHADOW, expired) and ZERO times with real capital — its `ACTIVE` `SETUP_STATUS` label is a backtest-only recommendation with no operational consequence, and is *currently flagged* by this session's own invariant-watcher output as a likely bug ("rated ACTIVE... but has ZERO real origin_status='ACTIVE' rows ever"). So there was no live-ACTIVE precedent to diverge from — the correct justification for forcing SHADOW is simply this codebase's own hard rule (never fire live before real N≥20), not a sweep-vs-post-mortem distinction. The decision (SHADOW) was right; the reasoning was fixed before building.

**Build**: `resolveSetupType()` override in `acd.js` (gap-down = today's 9:30 open below `lp.PD_CLOSE`, the prior session's RTH close). `scripts/backtest_or5_low_gap_down.mjs` — DeepSeek's review caught that the detection logic needed to be lifted from the mining script (15pt close-touch, first-touch-after-gate, from-above direction, `price_bars_primary`), NOT from `backtest_wpp_short_gap.mjs`'s different 5pt high/low-crossing convention, or the persisted numbers wouldn't match what was actually validated — confirmed by running it: reproduces N=147/EV=$7.77 (aligned) and N=194/EV=-$2.08 (against) exactly. Two real bugs caught by Claude's own test-run before trusting the numbers: `getOpeningRange()` needs a `time` string field the initial query omitted (0 touches generated silently), and `precomputeCrossovers()` reads `trade.direction` for MAE/MFE sign — omitting it silently treated every LONG trade as SHORT (wrong sign, wrong magnitude, no error thrown). `recommendation` is hardcoded `THIN_N` regardless of the backtest's own N/EV, with `notes` explaining why and that promotion to `ACTIVE` requires a deliberate manual step once real forward trades clear N≥20 — never automatic, unlike WPP's flawed pattern. `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS` entry (`setupTypes.js`, `skipGenericSetupStatus: true`, same overwrite-race protection as WPP), `setupDefinitions.js` entry, `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` suppressed-array entry, registered in `run_weekly_backtests.sh`.

**Separately found, fixed 2026-08-18**: the wider-target-mechanism ("trailing mechanism") thread's `wider_target_mult` column was only set on 3 of 4 SHADOW insert paths in `acd.js` — the "suppressed near-level audit" branch (the majority source of SHADOW rows, since most setup_types are suppressed by default) never got it wired, the same bug class already found and fixed for `runner_trail_width` on this identical code path. Fixed as part of the same-day wider-target bug-hunt entry above (commit `15a0502`) — see that entry for verification detail.

**Next**: run `node scripts/test_invariants.mjs`, confirm no regressions, then a DeepSeek code-review pass on the full diff before considering this done. Monitor `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG_GAP_DOWN` at real (`origin_status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')`) N=50 before any promotion discussion.

## ✅ 2026-08-17/18: per-trade wider-target counterfactual built on quick-check.html, a real live P&L bug found and fixed along the way

Direct continuation of the trailing-mechanism thread: after seeing the aggregate "Research & Monitoring" box (built earlier the same session — two lines showing the wider-target mechanism's backtest headline + live tally, now surfaced on both Home Assistant's feed and `quick-check.html`), the user asked for a per-trade version: "how much extra could this specific trade have gotten." Built `GET /api/setups/:id/wider-target-counterfactual` (re-walks one trade's real bars through the shared `stepWiderTarget()`, gated to `resolution='TARGET_HIT' AND bars_to_resolution<=4 AND wider_target_mult IS NULL AND extend_target_level IS NULL`) and a visually-separate, sign-neutral section in the trade-detail modal. Two full DeepSeek design-critique-then-code-review cycles, both catching real things.

**A real, live bug found while testing, not hypothetical**: `entry_zone_low`/`entry_zone_high`/`stop_level`/`t1_level` were never cast to `::float` in `resolveSetupsByPrice()`'s main SELECT — node-postgres returns NUMERIC columns as JS strings, and the wider-target branch's `entry + t1Distance * widerTargetMult` (LONG side) silently STRING-CONCATENATED instead of adding (`"29872" + 63` → `"2987263"`, not `29935`), producing a wider target so large it could never be reached — every LONG SHADOW row that armed the mechanism would have resolved `WIDER_TIME_EXPIRED` with a wrong mark-to-market `actual_pnl` instead of its real outcome. SHORT trades were accidentally safe (JS's `-` always numeric-coerces). Found by testing the read-only counterfactual endpoint against a real LONG trade (id 95409) before DeepSeek's second review pass flagged that the identical bug also existed in the live write path, not just the new endpoint. **Checked every affected row's real price bars by hand before fixing**: zero LONG SHADOW `wider_target_mult`-tagged trades had actually armed the buggy branch yet (none had touched T1 within the 4-bar window), so this was a live, loaded bug with zero realized corruption — a forward-looking fix, no backfill needed. Fixed by casting all 4 columns to `::float` in the SELECT, which also fixed two latent (currently-coincidentally-correct-by-uniform-digit-count) string comparisons in the same function.

Also fixed per the second DeepSeek review pass: an eligibility gate gap (STACK_VOL_BREAK's `extend_target_level` rows could pass every check and get compared as if they'd used the wider-target mechanism), a frontend stale-fetch race (the modal reuses the same element id across opens — a slow-resolving fetch for trade A could render into trade B's box if the user clicked through quickly), a UX trap where abnormal ineligibility reasons (data-integrity anomalies) were silently hidden identically to normal ones (just ineligible), and an `innerHTML`-with-server-strings hardening pass on the unrelated aggregate-note renderer.

**Process note**: this session hit two real infrastructure interruptions mid-build (a WSL crash mid-way through the first confirmation-pass dispatch, then the whole Claude Code session itself crashing during the retry) — both recovered cleanly because every fix was already written to disk before either crash, not held in any transient state. Re-dispatched DeepSeek fresh the next session-open and got a clean confirmation pass. Extracted `WIDER_TARGET_MULT`/`MAX_BARS_TO_T1_FOR_WIDER` as named constants (`widerTargetWalker.js`) along the way, replacing 4 independent `1.5`/`4` literals.

Verified: `test_invariants.mjs` identical 8/30 baseline (confirmed via `git stash`), `eslint` clean, server restarted multiple times across the session and re-tested live each time (including the exact previously-broken case), Playwright confirms no console errors and correct rendering for the eligible/too-slow/already-armed cases.

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (post-clear continuation, part 3): the 3 items flagged at the end of part 2 — full DeepSeek design-critique-then-code-review cycle

Direct continuation of part 2 below: the 3 items that batch left open (`islongsetup_bug_survives_in_3_other_files` HIGH, `isbearish_isbullish_heuristic_zone_edge_fade_gap` LOW, and the stale-worded Item 2c) per a direct user request to check/scope all 3, get a DeepSeek design critique before building, then a DeepSeek code-review pass after. Both DeepSeek passes caught real, non-cosmetic issues.

**Blast-radius measurement (done before scoping anything)**: queried the live DB directly rather than assuming. `setup_outcome_backtest` has 0 rows matching the affected setup_type pattern — the backtest endpoint has never actually run against one of these types. Of the 20 `active_setups` rows affected (1 `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP`, 19 `ZONE_EDGE_FADE`), only 15 have `mae_points`/`mfe_points` already computed, and all 15 happen to be direction-coincidentally-correct (14 `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` default-guessed SHORT and really are SHORT; the 1 `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` mismatch has no MAE/MFE computed yet). **Net: zero rows in the DB carried a wrong-signed value from this bug — no backfill decision needed, this was a forward-looking fix.**

**Item 1 (`islongsetup_bug_survives_in_3_other_files`) — fixed.** `resolveDirection()` (previously a local function in `acd.js`) extracted into `server/config/setupTypes.js` alongside `inferDirection()` — the canonical, shared home per that file's own header comment, and the fix all 3 downstream files now import instead of hand-rolling their own copy. **DeepSeek's design-critique found 2 real corrections to the plan**: (1) the "~38 standalone callers of `directionFromType`" estimate was wrong — the real count is 65 (44 `backtest_*`/`pilot_*`); (2) `scripts/backfill_mae_mfe.mjs` — a real DB-writing script — calls `directionFromType()` directly, not via `computeMaeMfe()` as originally assumed, and would have been left on the old buggy guess-for-`ZONE_EDGE_FADE` behavior under the original plan. Final design: `directionFromType()` in `maeMfeReplay.js` gets a minimal, signature-preserving fix (strip the `_GAP_*` suffix, same one-line pattern as `inferDirection()`) so all 65 name-only callers benefit for free; the 3 real full-row call sites (`computeMaeMfe()`, `backfill_mae_mfe.mjs`, and `setupBacktestService.js`'s `isLong()`) upgrade to the row-aware `resolveDirection()` with its price-derived fallback. `acd.js`'s dead `directionFromType` import (never actually called) was dropped in the same pass (DeepSeek-caught).

`caseEngine.js`'s two call sites needed different null-handling, and turned out to be a bigger fix than scoped: DeepSeek's critique correctly distinguished `checkSessionActivation()` (null must `return false` outright, since `isLong` drives a delta-direction comparison loop, not just a scoring bonus) from the trigger/impact-score block (null should skip only the direction-*dependent* adjustments, keeping the direction-independent "+1 validated setup type"). But DeepSeek's critique was scoped to the excerpt it was shown — building the fix surfaced that the trigger/impact block's `isLong` also drives a real **state machine** further down (`t1Hit`/`stopHit`/`premiseBroken` deciding `trigger.state` — ACTIVE/RESOLVED/WATCHING), including a second `if (latestSetup) trigger = {...}` statement that reconstructs the final `trigger` object from variables (`isLong`/`impact`/`triggerState`/etc) declared earlier in the same function. Selectively gating individual scoring lines while still running the state machine on a guessed direction would have risked reporting a wrong resolution (e.g. "stopped out" when the true direction actually hit target) for `ZONE_EDGE_FADE`/`_GAP_*` setups. Restructured to compute direction once up front and gate the whole block together. **Self-correction, DeepSeek's code-review pass**: my first write-up of this claimed the second statement was a separate SIBLING block outside the first — factually wrong, verified directly against the diff and indentation (it's nested INSIDE the first block, always was; if it had been a sibling the original pre-fix code would have thrown a `ReferenceError` on every run, which never happened). No latent scope bug existed either way — the code is correct — but the comment explaining *why* was rewritten to describe the real (nested, not sibling) structure before this was committed.

**Item 2 (`isbearish_isbullish_heuristic_zone_edge_fade_gap`) — fixed, confirmed the smallest of the three.** `acd.js`'s `structurallyInvalidateSetups()` had a THIRD hand-rolled direction heuristic (`isBearish`/`isBullish`, plus two dead `bearishPattern`/`bullishPattern` arrays never referenced anywhere) — despite `resolveDirection()` already being defined ~30 lines above in the same file and already called once in this same function for the pnl calc. Deleted the dead arrays and the heuristic functions; the invalidation gate now reuses the exact same `resolveDirection(row)` result already computed for the pnl calc (previously two separate calls per row — DeepSeek's code-review flagged the hoist). Closes a real coverage gap for free: `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` matched neither `isBearish` nor `isBullish`, so it was never structurally invalidated by this function at all. Null direction → `shouldInvalidate` stays false (confirmed as the right call — already this exact function's own established convention on the pnl side). The `isBracket` branch's separate `includes('LONG')` check was confirmed out of scope and safe (BRACKET_BREAKOUT only ships as `_LONG`/`_SHORT`, no `_GAP_*` variant exists).

**Item 3 (the stale "T1-floor, capped allocation" wording) — doc-only correction, per DeepSeek's own recommendation.** Re-confirmed the data-readiness finding from earlier: `WIDER_TARGET_LIVE_STATUS` shows `n_armed=1` (one day after shipping), nowhere near the mechanism's own N≥20 promotion bar — this remains correctly deferred, not a build task. Updated `docs/RUNNER_FOLLOWUPS_SPEC_20260817.md`'s Item 2c section (the literal "T1-floor structure, capped allocation" framing predates the same investigation's own tautology proof and was pointing a future session at building a mathematically meaningless mechanism) to instead correctly describe the already-shipped `stepWiderTarget()`/`wider_target_mult` mechanism and the real remaining work (an `ACTIVE`-eligibility gate + data-derived sizing) once real N clears the bar. Also flagged, for the next session that revisits this: the literal `rigor.clean=true` promotion criterion may be structurally hard to clear for this mechanism class specifically, since runner findings cluster on trend days by construction — cross-references `OPEN_DECISION computerigor_stable_clustered_independence_gap`.

**Code review, second pass**: DeepSeek's code-review of the built diff confirmed the code itself correct (all field-name contracts check out, the shared `resolveDirection()` extraction is sound) but caught 2 real things: (1) my own write-up factually mischaracterized the `caseEngine.js` structure as two sibling blocks needing a scope-hazard workaround — verified against the actual diff/indentation, the second statement was always nested inside the first (if it hadn't been, the pre-fix code would have thrown a `ReferenceError` on every run, which never happened) — no bug existed, but the misleading comment/doc narrative was rewritten before committing; (2) two more independent `_GAP_*`-unsafe direction heuristics exist outside this diff's scope (`CalendarView.jsx`'s `isLongSetupType()`, display-only, and `repair_dead_end_shadow_rows_20260727.mjs`'s `isLongSetup()`, a re-runnable DB-writing repair script) — flagged as a new low-priority `OPEN_DECISION gap_direction_bug_survives_calendarview_and_repair_script` rather than silently left unmentioned. Also added two cheap defensive `Number.isFinite` guards DeepSeek suggested (`resolveDirection()`'s price-fallback NaN edge, `computeMaeMfe()`'s pre-existing NaN-entry gap) — both optional, both consistent with the "exclude, don't guess" posture already established by this fix.

**Verification (actually run, not asserted in advance)**: `node scripts/test_invariants.mjs` — 8 FAILUREs / 30 WARNINGs, identical in count and content to a `git stash`-baseline run before any of tonight's changes (5 missing `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` rows + 3 `OPTIMAL_STOP` circuit-breaker trips, all pre-existing and unrelated). `npx eslint` clean across all 6 touched code files, `npm run build` clean (pre-existing chunk-size warning only). Server restarted (`fuser -k 3002/tcp` + `npm run server`); `GET /api/acd/setup-detection` and `GET /api/case?date=<today>` both return HTTP 200 — the latter exercised the rewritten `caseEngine.js` trigger block directly (confirmed `trigger.state`/`direction` populate correctly in the response, no crash). Watched through 2+ of the server's own 15-second auto-poll cycles (which call `structurallyInvalidateSetups()`, the function Item 2 touched, on every cycle) — zero new `scratch/server_errors.jsonl` entries.

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (post-clear continuation, part 2): 5-item runner follow-up batch — full spec in `docs/RUNNER_FOLLOWUPS_SPEC_20260817.md`

Picked up right after the DOW-gate fix (previous entry below), driven by a direct user request: run the wider-target auditor daily until real N breaks 20, and scope out the 3 pending follow-ups from the trailing-mechanism thread with DeepSeek reviewing both the scope and the final product. Given the user's explicit "let it continually review after each completion," every item went through its own design-critique-before-code and code-review-after-code DeepSeek pass, not one review at the end — 3 separate DeepSeek dispatches total (1 combined design critique for all 5 items, 1 combined code review for the 4 small items, 1 dedicated code review for the highest-stakes item). Both reviews caught real, non-cosmetic issues, consistent with every other DeepSeek-reviewed change tonight.

- **Item 0 (daily-audit throttle)**: `scripts/audit_wider_target_live.mjs` now accepts `--daily-check` (wired into `run_daily_calibration.sh`, Mon-Fri) — self-throttles the write/flag (not the computation) once real armed N has already cleared `MIN_N=20` as of the prior stored row, deferring to the existing weekly-only cadence with no manual cron revert needed later. **DeepSeek's code-review caught a real placement bug**: the throttle `return` was originally positioned before the meanDelta/verdict/rigor computation, meaning a throttled day would have skipped that computation entirely — contradicting the code's own comment promising it "still runs every day." Moved the `return` to right before the `performance_audit` INSERT, after the verdict is fully computed and logged, so the fix now does what it always claimed to.
- **Item 3 (`boundaryStraddle`)**: `computeRigor()` (`server/services/rigorDiagnostics.js`) now detects whether a chronological-stability check's dominant single-day entity straddles more than one of the 3 thirds (the specific 33-50%-single-entity contamination mode that can inflate a spurious `clean=true`). Informational only — `clean`'s own semantics are untouched, deliberately, since tightening it would ripple into the `SETUP_STATUS_DOW` gate fixed earlier the same session. Surfaced in `rigorContext()` and, per DeepSeek's review, in the 2 inline notes objects that actually gate live decisions (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s main gate + DOW sub-pass) — found and fixed a real bug myself while verifying this: a local `rigorDiagnostics(type)` wrapper in that file was silently trimming the new field before it could reach those objects, caught by re-running and noticing the field missing from a live row, not by code inspection alone.
- **Item 2a (visibility tile)**: extended the existing `RunnerWiderTargetPanel`/"Live mechanism results" card (`AlphaEngineOverview.jsx`) rather than building a new one — added day-sign-majority, survives-largest-day-exclusion, and dominant-setup_type-share (all already computed and stored, just never surfaced), plus an explicit nArmed-toward-20 progress bar distinct from the pre-existing full-live-promotion bar (different population/purpose).
- **Item 2b (negative-trace process rule)**: new CLAUDE.md bullet + `docs/CONVENTIONS_DETAIL.md#negative-trace-rule-for-runner-findings` — a negative verdict on a runner/winner-extension finding must cite a specific TESTED mechanism failure, not an inference from a different pipeline's result (the exact mistake that produced this same thread's own false wider-target closure earlier the same session it was corrected). `CLOSED_INFERRED`/`CLOSED_TESTED` marker introduced for future claim text; the adversarial stress-test battery is now the default gate before recording a positive runner finding, not just after a challenge.
- **Item 2c (bounded-live wider-target mechanism, mislabeled "T1-floor" at the time this bullet was written — see the 2026-08-17 part-3 entry above for the correction)**: deliberately scope-only, not built — real dependency on Item 1 landing first (extends the same `resolveSetupsByPrice()` direction logic), plus its own higher blast radius (real ACTIVE-origin positions) and the full new-setup-type checklist. DeepSeek's design-critique sanity-checked and confirmed this deferral reasoning sound.
- **Item 1 (`isLongSetup` direction-inference fix, the highest-stakes item)**: resolves `OPEN_DECISION islongsetup_gap_variant_direction_bug`. Real scope correction found during research — the bug's blast radius was 4 call sites across 3 functions (`resolveSetupsByPrice()` ×2, `expireStaleSetups()`, `structurallyInvalidateSetups()`), not 2 across 1 as originally flagged, and it also silently misclassified all 19 historical `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` rows as SHORT unconditionally (a related bug — `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` has no name-encoded direction for even the correct `inferDirection()` to find). **DeepSeek's design-critique caught something no amount of code review would have found without it**: `acd.js` had its own LOCAL `inferDirection()` (line 355) — different from and equally buggy as the canonical `setupTypes.js` version — that would have silently shadowed the fix if the import had been added without deleting it. Verified independently before trusting it. Final design (hybrid, both DeepSeek passes): canonical `inferDirection()` as primary signal, price-derived direction (`t1_level > stop_level`, `Number()`-coerced per a real latent numeric-string-comparison bug the code-review caught) as fallback for name-directionless types, `null`/exclude on disagreement. ## ✅ 2026-08-17 (post-clear continuation): SETUP_STATUS_DOW's live SUPPRESS gate rescoped to real data — resolves `setup_status_dow_gate_93pct_synthetic_never_rescoped`

Picked up as the highest-priority pending item flagged at the end of the prior session (found while investigating the wider-target mechanism's `computeRigor()` methodology, unrelated to that thread itself). `scripts/backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s per-weekday suppression sub-pass (`SETUP_STATUS_DOW`) never got the main gate's 2026-08-10 real-data-scoping fix — it was still gating a live trading-suppression decision on the blended (mostly synthetic `BACKFILL`) population. Confirmed directly against the DB before touching anything: of 267 `(setup_type, dow)` cells clearing blended N≥20, only 8 also clear `real_n≥20` (135 have `real_n=0`) — the exact ~93%-synthetic contamination CLAUDE.md's `origin_status` hard rule exists to catch, just never migrated to this second pass.

**Fixed**: the DOW sub-pass now mirrors the main gate's real_n/real_ev three-way branch exactly (`real_n<20 → THIN_N`, `real_n≥20 AND real_ev<-$5 AND rigor.clean (computed on the REAL-only trade population) → SUPPRESS`, else `→ ACTIVE`).
