Extracted a single shared `REAL_TRADE_FILTER` constant (used at all 9 call sites in the file — main gate's 3 queries + the DOW pass's 2) specifically because the duplicated, un-synced filter string was the actual drift mechanism that caused this bug — a future fix to one now can't miss the other. Went through the full design-critique → implement → code-review DeepSeek workflow (both passes: "ship it, no real bugs" — the design critique flagged 3 adjustments, all applied; the code review flagged one hardening note, the shared-constant extraction, also applied).

**Verified directly against the live DB, not just script output**: post-fix, `SETUP_STATUS_DOW` rows are `{THIN_N: 230, ACTIVE: 34, SUPPRESS: 0}` — the 32 previously-live weekday suppressions built on synthetic data are gone, correctly replaced by honest THIN_N pending real data. Spot-checked `IB_BEARISH` (the one DOW suppression with a named live-incident history, "Thu×IB_BEARISH EV=-$17 N=27 suppressed as of 2026-07-09"): Thursday correctly lifted to THIN_N (real_n=3); Friday correctly stayed non-suppressed despite real_n=53/real_ev=-$9.47 clearing both bars, because rigor caught genuine day-clustering (all 53 real trades land on ≤5 distinct trading days). `test_invariants.mjs` shows the same 8 pre-existing FAILUREs as before this change (confirmed via `git stash`, all unrelated — 5 missing `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` rows, 3 `OPTIMAL_STOP` circuit breaker trips). Server restarted (`liveStats`/`_dowSuppressToday` sits behind a 12hr `DAY_CACHE_TTL`, same reason the 2026-08-16 cascade-breaker repair needed a restart) and verified healthy — no new `SERVER_ERROR`/`CLIENT_ERROR` entries post-restart.

**Not a malfunction, the intended effect**: the DOW overlay going mostly-THIN_N is the honest state of the data — real weekday-granularity trade history is thin almost everywhere. It self-heals the same way the main gate does (next weekly/daily cron run auto-recomputes as real N grows), just slower, since DOW real-N accumulation is throttled by the setup's own global ACTIVE/SHADOW firing rate rather than an independent path. The separate "13-of-32 one-trade-margin rigor fragility" finding from the original investigation is now moot — those 32 blended-SUPPRESS cells mostly don't even reach the real_n≥20 gate anymore.

~~**Also confirmed while triaging session start**: the systemd unit `trading-journal-server.service` is in a crash-looped `failed` state (16h+ old, "Start request repeated too quickly") — but the app itself was actually up and serving fine via a leftover `restart.sh`/nodemon dev session from before the session's `/clear`, not an active outage. Left as-is (now restarted cleanly as part of this fix) rather than intervened on mid-investigation; worth a look next session if the systemd unit should be reconciled/re-enabled instead of relying on the ad hoc dev session.~~ **Resolved 2026-08-18**: this exact drift (`restart.sh` missing the systemd-stop/handback logic `start.sh` has) is precisely what accumulated into 5 duplicate server supervisors and a real RTH detection gap the same day — see the top entry in this file. Root-caused and fixed structurally, not just restarted again.

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (closed-loop monitoring + Home Assistant): the wider-target mechanism now has a real recheck/promotion path — and a real pre-existing bug got found and scoped along the way

The user pointed out a real gap after the mechanism build below shipped: it runs and writes real outcomes, but nothing was reading that data back or deciding whether it's working — a genuine "no dead ends" violation, not a hypothetical one. Fixed with the same design→scope→implement→QA→review sequence as everything else tonight (three separate DeepSeek passes: design, scope, final code review).

**Design**: the counterfactual baseline turned out to be deterministic — since `stepWiderTarget()` only arms on a clean T1 touch (no same-bar stop conflict), the fixed-target exit the trade would otherwise have taken is exactly `|t1_level − entry| × $/pt − commission`, computable straight from the stored row with no bar-replay needed. Pass bar: N≥20 armed + mean delta > 0 + a clean `computeRigor` pass, with 4 states (`THIN_N`/`REPRODUCED`/`CONTRADICTED`/`UNSTABLE`) — deliberately dropping the historical backtest's family-exclusion gate (not a live selection-bias risk here) while still reporting it informationally. A verdict *transition* (not every check) flags a human decision — `REPRODUCED` surfaces "consider building the ACTIVE-row wiring," `CONTRADICTED` surfaces "consider killing it" — never automatic, and structurally can't touch real capital regardless (`ACTIVE` rows can't carry the flag at all).

**Scope review caught a real, pre-existing bug** before any code was written: `resolveSetupsByPrice()`'s direction helper (`isLongSetup()`) misclassifies `_GAP_UP`/`_GAP_DOWN` conditional variants — confirmed concretely, `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` is a real SHORT trade that this helper reads as LONG (bare `_UP` substring match, no gap-suffix stripping, unlike the more correct `inferDirection()`). This isn't new — it affects every branch of the live resolution function, not just the new one — but the monitoring script inherits its consequences and had to be built defensively around it: any armed row where the two direction functions disagree gets excluded and reported, never silently folded into the mean. Flagged separately as `OPEN_DECISION islongsetup_gap_variant_direction_bug` (the actual fix is a larger, separate, higher-blast-radius change, deliberately not done here).

**Built**: `scripts/audit_wider_target_live.mjs` (new `WIDER_TARGET_LIVE_STATUS` signal_type, wired into `run_weekly_backtests.sh`'s audit-pipelines section — the frozen backtest `RESEARCH_CLAIM` stays untouched as historical reference, this is a separate, live-recomputed signal), an extended `GET /acd/runner-wider-target-status` (new `liveMechanism` field) and a new "Live mechanism results" card in the dashboard tile, and — per the user's explicit request — two new lines in `GET /setups/today-summary` (already Home Assistant's existing "Session Timeline" feed, no new tunnel config needed): an always-shown research headline, and a live tally line that only appears once real trades have actually fired through the mechanism.

**Final code review caught one more real thing**: the script stored `win_rate` on a 0–100 scale, violating this codebase's own standing convention (always 0–1) — harmless today only because its two current consumers happened to agree with each other, but it would have silently double-multiplied once the generic Unified Signal Table read this signal_type's `win_rate` and applied its own `×100`. Fixed before committing.

Verified end to end: `test_invariants.mjs` shows zero new failures, lint/build clean, all 4 touched endpoints return 200, a real browser check confirms the new dashboard card renders, and the HA feed correctly shows the always-on line while the conditional live-tally line stays correctly absent (zero real trades have fired through the mechanism yet — expected, right after build).

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (mechanism build): the wider-target runner mechanism is now live, SHADOW-only — two rounds of DeepSeek review caught a wrong risk shape and a wrong insert-site guess before either shipped

Direct continuation of the strategic close-out below: the user asked to build the actual mechanism the cross-family-validated finding earned. Per this codebase's own rule for anything touching the live resolution engine, got a design critique from DeepSeek before writing code — and it found Claude's own framing was inverted.

**Round 1 (design)**: the "bounded-live" language used throughout this thread described a T1-floor exit (bank T1 as a guaranteed floor, never lose fresh capital) — but DeepSeek proved that shape is a mathematical tautology: its PnL is ≥ the T1-baseline on every single trade by construction, so a "positive" backtest of it would prove nothing, the same weak-dominance trap already documented for the "bank-at-T1-then-trail" arm earlier in this thread. Built the actual comparison (`scratch/velocity_wider_target_t1floor_vs_origstop.mjs`) and confirmed empirically: 0 of 292 T1-floor trades ever showed a negative delta. The version worth building was the one actually backtested — wider target, original stop, real flip-to-loss risk (43 of 292 negative) — which DeepSeek also identified as structurally identical to an already-live mechanism (`STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE_*`'s bank-vs-extend branch), not a novel risk shape.

Built per DeepSeek's 6-step spec: a shared pure step function (`server/services/widerTargetWalker.js`, 17/17 synthetic tests, mirroring `breakevenTrailWalker.js`'s own "provable about the live path itself" convention), a new nullable `active_setups.wider_target_mult` column (flag + multiplier value, same convention as `runner_trail_width`/`extend_target_level`), and a 4th mutually-exclusive branch in `resolveSetupsByPrice()` gated on `barCount<=4` — confirmed to exactly match how `bars_to_resolution` itself gets written, avoiding an off-by-one DeepSeek flagged in advance between the branch's own bar-counting convention and the stored column's.

**Round 2 (insert-site review)**: before wiring which live INSERT sites actually set the flag, Claude proposed `{1,5,6,8}` (Globex, RTH backfill, main RTH candidates, momentum) — sent it to DeepSeek rather than guessing, and the review found it wrong on both ends. The real answer is `{5,6,7}`: it **omitted site 7** (`shadowCandidates`), which turned out to be the *dominant* source of the SHADOW-bucket evidence — the `VWAP_MAGNET`/`GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET` families (59% of the cross-family validation) fire through site 7 during RTH hours, not through the Globex insert as the naming suggested (verified via actual hour distribution and null-column patterns, not variable names). And it **wrongly included site 1** (Globex — not where the validated families actually fire) and **site 8** (momentum — zero real `active_setups` rows ever). The review also caught real safety requirements Claude would have missed: site 6 needed an explicit `!isTrailMechanism` exclusion (its SHADOW gate is also true for trail variants, which already carry `runner_trail_width` from the same INSERT), site 7 needed an `ABSORPTION_LONG`/`COIL_SURGE` exclusion (their own custom resolution), and every site's new column/parameter had to be strictly append-only (the existing `VALUES` lists use a `$N`-offset scheme keyed to `REGIME_STAMP_COLS`/`FIRE_TAG_COLS` lengths — a middle insertion would have silently renumbered and corrupted every parameter after it).

Wired sites 5, 6, 7 exactly per the corrected spec — hand-verified parameter-count/index alignment on all three afterward (not just trusted). `test_invariants.mjs` shows zero new failures (all pre-existing, confirmed by content, not just count); lint/build clean; live server restarted under nodemon with no new errors; `GET /api/acd/setup-detection` confirmed error-free; a direct Postgres transaction dry-run round-tripped the new column correctly. The visibility tile (built earlier the same day) now reflects `mechanismBuilt: true`, live-verified.

**Real, verified safety property, not assumed**: `status` is never promoted `SHADOW`→`ACTIVE` for an already-open row after insert — every `UPDATE active_setups SET status=` site only ever sets `RESOLVED` or `EXPIRED` — so the origin/status value computed once at insert time is permanent for the row's whole life, and `ACTIVE` (real-capital) rows are structurally unreachable by this mechanism, not just unreachable at the moment of insert.

**Still SHADOW-only, not promoted** — this build makes real forward data accumulation possible, it doesn't itself constitute validation. Full detail and remaining follow-ons (a documented negative-trace process rule, optionally extending to the Globex/momentum sites later) tracked in `OPEN_DECISION runner_bounded_live_gate_passed_needs_build_and_process`.

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (strategic close-out): the runner-trail/wider-target thread lands on a real, cross-family-validated finding — plus a decision framework for how this codebase should treat "runner" research going forward

The user pushed back directly on the pattern this whole thread had fallen into — "we need to improve runners we can't let rigor keep killing us" — and asked DeepSeek for an actual strategic recommendation, not another single-finding stress-test. DeepSeek's answer, verbatim theme: **don't lower the rigor bar, re-specify it** — the raw `top5DayPct>50%` clustering check is genuinely wrong for this *class* of finding (it conflates "one setup sliced many ways," the CAM-bug pattern it was built to catch, with "momentum-selected trades naturally cluster on trend days," which is structural to any runner finding by construction), and the earlier `ACTIVE`-only scoping (N=77) was itself inconsistent with this codebase's own standard real-data convention (`ACTIVE`+`SHADOW`) used everywhere else.

Acted on the highest-leverage recommendation immediately: re-scoped the wider-target resimulation to `ACTIVE`+`SHADOW` (N=292 — 77 ACTIVE + 215 SHADOW, the SHADOW half dominated by a completely different setup family, `VWAP_MAGNET`, than the ACTIVE half's `IB_BEARISH`) and ran DeepSeek's proposed 4-gate "bounded-live" test in place of the raw clustering bit: N≥20 (PASS, 292), chronological stability via `computeRigor` (PASS, all three thirds positive), a day-sign test — majority of distinct days positive AND survives excluding the single largest day (PASS — 22/26 days positive, and excluding 07-29 the mean *increases* to $12.47) — and a dominant-family-exclusion guard, N≥20 and positive after dropping the largest family (PASS — excluding `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET` leaves N=217, mean $14.21). **All four pass.** This is a genuinely cross-family, cross-day replication, not the same population re-sliced — the blended mean delta is $11.86/trade (lower than the ACTIVE-only $27.95, and more honest, since it's diluted across ~39 setup_type families instead of dominated by one). A handful of large-magnitude single-trade negative cells (e.g. `PD_HIGH_FADE_SHORT` −$284) were checked directly against raw entry/stop/target levels — confirmed real flip-to-loss cases (unusually wide original stops relative to target), not data artifacts.

Upgraded `RESEARCH_CLAIM velocity_fast_wider_target_positive_provisional` in place (same slug, CONFIRMED) and resolved `OPEN_DECISION velocity_fast_wider_target_needs_rigor_pass_before_shadow` with the full result. **Still not a live-change candidate** — clears DeepSeek's proposed intermediate "bounded-live" rung (capped, give-back-only risk), not the "full live" bar (which still requires `computeRigor` `clean=true` at N≥20 real, the same standard as entry signals — the raw clustering check still trips here, now an explicit, accepted limitation of that specific test for this class, tracked in `OPEN_DECISION computerigor_stable_clustered_independence_gap`).

**Three concrete items remain, not built tonight**, tracked in `OPEN_DECISION runner_bounded_live_gate_passed_needs_build_and_process`: (1) build the actual bounded-live mechanism (T1-floor structure, capped allocation, reusing existing breakeven-trail machinery — a real live-code-touching build needing the full new-setup-type checklist and a DeepSeek design pass before writing the live path); (2) a visibility tile in `AlphaEngineOverview.jsx` ("PROVISIONAL — under evaluation," headline delta, live N, gate statuses, N-of-20 progress bar) — cheapest item, zero statistical risk, directly answers the "this feels like a black box" complaint; (3) a "negative-trace" process rule for future runner-class findings — a negative must cite a specific *tested* mechanism failure with a number (matching the trail thread's genuine "transient spike, measured, two shapes, full sweep" answer), never an inferred/borrowed result from a different pipeline — the exact mistake that produced this thread's own false wider-target closure earlier the same session.

**Where this leaves the broader question**: the trail-mechanism half of "let winners run" is a real, decisively-earned negative (transient spike, not a continuation — no trail captures it). The wider-target half is a real, now cross-family-validated positive, currently capped at the SHADOW/bounded-live tier pending the actual mechanism build. Neither is "rigor killing a runner" — the process worked, but it took the user pushing back on an over-hasty closure, and a re-specified test that measures the right threat instead of a blunt proxy, to get there.

## 🔴 2026-08-17 (spun off the wider-target thread): SETUP_STATUS_DOW's live SUPPRESS/ACTIVE gate is deciding on a ~93% synthetic population — never got the 2026-08-10 real-scoping fix the main gate received

Asked DeepSeek a systemic follow-up after the `computeRigor()` tension in the wider-target thread below (does the "dominant day inflates both clustering AND stability checks at once" failure mode generalize to other live-gating consumers). The specific mechanism check came back reassuring — DeepSeek ran it directly against all 32 currently-`SUPPRESS`ed weekday cells in the live `SETUP_STATUS_DOW` gate and found zero instances (no dominant day holds ≥33% of N, sign never flips on leave-one-day-out). `computeCorrectedTarget()` is confirmed dormant (zero setup_types currently use `method='corrected-resim'`), so it's not deciding anything live today either.

**But checking that surfaced something bigger and unrelated to the original question**: `backtest_setup_status.mjs`'s DOW sub-pass (`dowQ`/`dowTradesQ`, ~lines 542-566) has no `origin_status` filter — unlike the main per-setup_type gate, fixed 2026-08-10 to real-only. Its population is 85% BACKFILL + 8% unrecoverable UNKNOWN = **93% non-real**, only 2% ACTIVE + 5% SHADOW. This is live, not hypothetical: **32 weekday cells are currently SUPPRESSED and 93 negative-EV weekday cells are currently firing ACTIVE**, all decided on this heavily-synthetic population — exactly the class of contamination CLAUDE.md's own `active_setups` hard rule warns about, applied to a real live gate rather than a reporting figure. Flagged `OPEN_DECISION setup_status_dow_gate_93pct_synthetic_never_rescoped` (HIGH) — not yet fixed, since real N per DOW cell may be too thin once BACKFILL/UNKNOWN are excluded, which needs checking before committing to a fix.

Also flagged the underlying `computeRigor()` methodology gap itself as a separate, lower-priority item: `clustered` (day-level, 50% threshold) and `stable` (event-position thirds) aren't independent — a dominant entity holding ~33-50% of events can straddle a chronological-third boundary and inflate `stable` while staying under the clustering bar, producing a spurious `clean=true`. Confirmed generic (not specific to this session's population) but directionally one-sided (can only produce a false "trust this," never a false suppress) and not currently causing any live problem (verified against all 3 real decision-gating consumers). `OPEN_DECISION computerigor_stable_clustered_independence_gap` (MEDIUM) — a cheap `boundaryStraddle` check was sketched but not built, worth doing before this migrates from an informational surface into a live gate (nearly happened this session with the velocity=Fast finding below).

## 🟡 2026-08-17 (same-day reversal): the wider-target closure below was itself wrong — a real, day-consistent positive survived an adversarial stress-test of its own proof

The closure directly below ("wider-target thread closed before any code was written") stood for under an hour before the user asked for a deep adversarial critique of the closure's OWN reasoning, not just a re-confirmation. DeepSeek found its own "already answered negative" pillar was wrong on three independent counts: (1) **category error** — `computeCorrectedTarget()`'s `failed_oos_or_baseline` tests a *joint* stop+target re-fit, not "widen the target, keep the stop," a different question than the one this thread is about; (2) **the actual direct wider-target test already on record found the OPPOSITE result** — `WIDER_TARGET_EV_TEST` (2026-07-19) shows `IB_BEARISH`/`IB_BULLISH` positive at +$67.36/+$42.61 at 2x, not negative (run once, dirty — no `origin_status` filter, includes 1,493 BACKFILL trades — never re-run clean, which is exactly what should have been done instead of citing an unrelated pipeline's result); (3) **the "negative" cited wasn't even stable** — `IB_BULLISH` passed cleanly with a 2x target at N=166 (`clean:true`) before a later `origin_status`-scoping fix collapsed its population to N≈60 and flipped the read. The day-clustering argument used to dismiss the fast bucket was also circular — driven by the same day-concentration used to reject the fast bucket in the first place.

Ran the actual cheap test that should have been run: reused `scripts/backtest_wider_target_ev.mjs`'s exact `resimulate()` logic (order-aware bar-walk, target-before-stop, verbatim, not reimplemented) correctly scoped to `origin_status='ACTIVE' AND bars_to_resolution<=4` (`scratch/velocity_fast_wider_target_scoped.mjs`), each trade's own `t1_level` distance as the widening anchor. **Result: fast bucket (N=77) shows a real, day-consistent positive** — 1.5x target: mean +$27.95/trade, median +$37.00, 73/77 still won (only 4 flipped to loss), 13/15 days positive (p=0.004). The non-fast comparison bucket (N=133, same code path, cleanly distributed) shows essentially nothing (+$3.53/-$0.75, ~coin-flip day fraction) — a genuine differential, not "wider targets are generally good."

DeepSeek stress-tested this reversal with the same adversarial rigor and it survived: not an `IB_BEARISH`/07-29 artifact (checked directly, not assumed — the effect holds excluding `IB_BEARISH` alone, N=36, +$19.50; excluding the entire IB fade family, N=24, +$29.63; excluding 07-29, N=50, +$25.04; excluding the whole 3-day cluster, N=34, +$25.51), no bug found (verified P&L matches `actual_pnl` exactly, entry-zone width is 0 for all 77 fast trades so no ambiguity, window never truncates the fast bucket). DeepSeek also named why its own prior was wrong, not just that it was: it conflated "55% of fast trades snap back to breakeven within the same bar" (a harmless wiggle) with "hits the stop" (the only thing that flips this specific test — 4/77 at 1.5x), and it applied an aggregate base rate to a population that's momentum-selected by construction, where that base rate doesn't transfer.

**Not yet actionable** — recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM velocity_fast_wider_target_positive_provisional` (PROVISIONAL, not CONFIRMED) because a `computeRigor()`/chronological-stability sweep hasn't been run on this specific result yet, N=77 spans one 5-week window only (regime risk untested), and the non-IB-family surviving subset is only N=24. The prior closure claim (`velocity_fast_wider_target_closed_untestable`) was corrected in place (marked `STALE`/superseded, not deleted, per this doc's own convention) rather than left standing. New `OPEN_DECISION velocity_fast_wider_target_needs_rigor_pass_before_shadow` tracks the concrete next steps: run `computeRigor()` properly, then (if clean) wire in as SHADOW-only at 1.5x (better risk-adjusted than 2x — similar mean delta, far fewer flip-to-loss trades) once real forward N≥20 accumulates — never a direct live change.

**Process lesson worth keeping**: closing a thread on an *inferred* negative (citing a related-but-different existing result) is not the same as closing it on a *tested* negative, and needs the same scrutiny as any other conclusion. This reversal only happened because the user explicitly declined to accept the closure's reasoning at face value.

## ✅ 2026-08-17 (immediate follow-up, SUPERSEDED — see entry above): wider-target thread closed before any code was written — DeepSeek's phase-0 design critique found the velocity axis is actually one setup_type on one day

Direct continuation of the trailing-stop closure below: since a trail can't capture the transient post-T1 spike, the natural next mechanism was a fixed wider target (doesn't require price to still be favorable when checked, unlike a trail). Per this codebase's own phase-0 convention, sent the design question to DeepSeek *before* writing any code — and it found, via two read-only SELECTs, that the whole premise doesn't hold: the fast/ACTIVE population (`bars_to_resolution<=4`, N=77) is 53% `IB_BEARISH`, and `IB_BEARISH`'s own fast trades are 66% concentrated on a single day (2026-07-29). Every other setup_type in the bucket has N≤12. "Velocity=Fast" isn't a general axis at this population size — it's substantially "IB_BEARISH on 07-29."

Worse for the wider-target idea specifically: the question has already been asked and answered, velocity-blind, by the existing live `computeCorrectedTarget()` pipeline (`server/services/targetCalibrationService.js`, wired into `scripts/update_optimal_stops.mjs`) — `IB_BEARISH`'s live `OPTIMAL_STOP` row already shows `exclusionReason='failed_oos_or_baseline'` (oosEv=-$13.28 vs fullEv=+$17.77 vs baselineEv=+$11.23, the canonical in-sample-fits/held-out-reverses shape the OOS guardrail exists to catch), and `IB_BULLISH` fails the same way. A wider target is already rejected for 69% of the fast bucket without any velocity conditioning at all. Both facts independently verified directly against the DB before recording anything (not just trusted from DeepSeek's summary).

Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM velocity_fast_wider_target_closed_untestable` (CONFIRMED) — closed as untestable on current data, not negative or positive, with a concrete 3-part reopen condition (≥3 setup_types with N≥20 each, T1-normalized excursion clears `computeRigor`'s clustering check, at least one type not already `failed_oos_or_baseline`) so this doesn't get quietly re-attempted the same way before the population composition actually changes.

**This closes the entire "let winners run past T1" investigation for now** — both mechanism classes tried (trail, fixed wider target), both hit real, verified walls, not assumed ones. The room-to-run phenomenon itself remains real and worth revisiting once real trade volume grows past the current concentration.

## ✅ 2026-08-17: runner-trail thread CLOSED for the trailing-stop mechanism family — room-to-run is real, but it's a transient spike no trail can capture (4 rounds of DeepSeek review, one new thread spun off)

Continuation of the round-7 entry directly below. The velocity=Fast axis (entry-to-T1 speed, `bars_to_resolution<=4`) was the one lead that survived setup_type/time_of_day/A-level all failing or being unusable — but it needed real vetting before trusting it, and got it: 4 separate DeepSeek review passes, each one catching something real.

**Pass 1 (design critique)**: caught two script bugs in the first rigor-check attempt — missing chronological sort before `computeRigor()` (so the "stable" flag wasn't testing time at all), and a velocity-tercile bucket that didn't match the one the recorded claim's numbers actually came from (3 different "Fast" bucket definitions were in play simultaneously). Both fixed exactly per DeepSeek's suggested fixes.

**Pass 2 (DeepSeek's own prediction)**: predicted the fixed velocity finding was a day-clustering artifact — 43.7% of the Fast bucket turned out to be the same 4 `*_MAGNET` setup types already flagged as artifacts in round 7, concentrated on 07-29/07-30. Also caught a real reasoning error in Claude's own framing: "the same 67.8% clustering shows up in two different metric formulations" was a tautology, not evidence — `computeRigor()`'s clustering check never reads the excursion metric, so identical clustering was guaranteed by construction, not informative.

**Pass 3 (DeepSeek falsifies its own prediction)**: built the exact structural test DeepSeek prescribed (leave-N-days-out, artifact-exclusion, fast-vs-non-fast as the correct control instead of fast-vs-everyone) and the result contradicted DeepSeek's own stated predictions — the day-equal-weighted delta stayed positive and stable (~$30-38/trade) across every exclusion cut, including the harshest one (dropping all `*_MAGNET*` types). Sent this reversal back to DeepSeek rather than trusting a surprising positive any more readily than a surprising negative. DeepSeek re-verified independently (re-ran the script, rebuilt with its own queries, tested a matched-same-day baseline) and confirmed: its own prediction was wrong. Day-level significance on the after-T1-only excursion metric: 20/25 days positive, p=0.004, bootstrap CI [24.3, 83.5] excluding zero — and the effect holds even comparing fast vs. non-fast on the *same calendar day*, which rules out "this is just a trending-day regime effect."

**Pass 4 (the actual tradeable question)**: room-to-run being real doesn't mean a trail can capture it — the prior metric was a peak (cumulative running-max), not captured P&L. Simulated the real live `stepBreakevenTrail()` mechanism on ACTIVE-only real trades: fast bucket lost -$44.12/trade vs. the fixed-T1-exit baseline (median -$70.00, 0/15 days positive, N=71). A result this clean got the same scrutiny as the round-2 negative — sent back to DeepSeek, which found the negative was real but the test was answering the wrong question. Root cause: 55% of fast/ACTIVE trades hit T1 and snap back to breakeven *within the same 1-minute bar* they touched it (vs. 7% for slow trades) — this happens before the trail width is ever consulted, and sweeping the width 0.5x-6x ATR left the fast median pinned at -$70 throughout. The reconciling diagnostic: fast trades genuinely peak higher post-T1 (248pt vs 164pt) but also reverse proportionally deeper (143pt vs 108pt adverse, 39% fully round-trip to entry-or-below) — **the extra room is a spike that reverses, not a sustained continuation**, so no trailing stop of any shape or width can harvest it (confirmed with a second mechanism too: banking T1 as a hard floor then trailing comes back flat, median exactly $0.00 at every width — protects the baseline, captures nothing extra).

**Net conclusion**: the "let winners run via a trailing exit" mechanism class is genuinely closed, across every axis tested this round (`day_type`/BALANCE negative, `setup_type` artifacts, `time_of_day` nothing, A-level unusable, velocity real-but-untrappable) — not a premature kill, a thoroughly cross-examined one. Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM velocity_fast_trail_negative_room_to_run_transient` (CONFIRMED) and resolved `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days`. **One new thread spun off, not started**: a fixed wider target (T2 — a different mechanism class than a trail, since it doesn't require price to still be favorable when checked) could in principle capture a spike a trail cannot. The older `WIDER_TARGET_EV_TEST` thread (2026-07-18, thin backtest-only positive on a different fat-tail fade subset) was never connected to this velocity=Fast population — that's the concrete starting point if "let winners run" is revisited, not another trail variant.

Also found along the way, unrelated to the trail question but real: `OPEN_DECISION a_level_time_columns_never_populated` — `acd_daily_log.a_up_time`/`a_down_time` are always NULL DB-wide, silently degrading a live dashboard chip (`acd.js`'s "A Up/A Down fired" detail text has never shown a time suffix) and `caseEngine.js`'s `firedAt` field (always null wherever read). Not fixed this session, flagged for whenever A-level timing is next needed.

## 🟡 2026-08-16 (4th follow-up session, round 7): the converged forward-return check ran — mixed result, one new real data-gap found, one genuinely promising lead (velocity), two axes shown to be traps

Executed the concrete next action both Gemini and DeepSeek converged on in round 6: a stop-free forward-return premise check on post-T1 continuation, sliced by setup_type/time-of-day/entry-to-T1 velocity/A-level alignment instead of `day_type`. Dispatched to Gemini (`scripts/backtest_post_t1_continuation_slices.mjs`), which built a real, structurally sound script (reused `inferDirection()`, the reference script's bar-walk/no-lookahead helpers, and a properly data-derived velocity-tercile split) and a clean 1,105-row CSV — but `scratch/antigravity_response.md` itself came back visibly corrupted (two different write attempts interleaved mid-sentence), a concrete instance of the standing "never trust the writeup, read the actual script/CSV" rule earning its keep again.

Direct audit (not just re-reading Gemini's summary) found:
1. **A-level axis is unusable, not just thin.** 100% of both the 5,094-trade pooled population and the 648-trade real-only population classified as "NEITHER" — zero variance. Root-caused: `acd_daily_log.a_up_time`/`a_down_time` are ALWAYS NULL DB-wide (0 of 392 real fired rows populated, confirmed directly), and no INSERT/UPDATE anywhere in `server/` ever writes them. This is a real, previously-unknown, structural gap — not a script bug, Gemini's join logic was correct given the data available. Two live consumers were silently degraded by it the whole time: `acd.js`'s "A Up/A Down fired" dashboard chip has never shown a time suffix, and `caseEngine.js`'s `firedAt` field has always resolved to null wherever it's read. Flagged `OPEN_DECISION a_level_time_columns_never_populated` (MEDIUM).
2. **setup_type axis's two striking-looking "wins" are day-clustering artifacts.** `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` and `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG` (real-only) both showed a clean monotonically-growing positive delta with horizon in Gemini's CSV — independently re-queried directly against `active_setups` and found `GLOBEX_VWAP_MAGNET_LONG`'s N=51 spans only 2 distinct real trading days (100% top5Share), `VWAP_MAGNET_LONG`'s N=48 spans 7 days (95.8% top5Share). This is the exact trap this whole thread has been routing around by avoiding `day_type` as the primary axis — it resurfaced via `setup_type` slicing instead. Do not act on these.
3. **Velocity axis is the one real, reasonably-distributed lead.** The bottom tercile of `bars_to_resolution` (fastest entry-to-T1 trades) shows a clean, monotonically GROWING positive delta vs. the unconditional baseline in real-only data: +5.37pt (15-bar horizon) → +11.53 → +16.76 → +26.29 → +33.92pt (240-bar). N=261 across 26 distinct real trading days (independently re-verified), not dominated by one setup_type (top type is 16.9% of the bucket). Unlike `day_type`, a trade's own bars-to-resolution-so-far is directly observable live as it happens — genuinely actionable if it survives further rigor checking.
4. **time_of_day axis showed nothing horizon-persistent** in real-only data.
5. **Methodology flag for future use of this dataset**: ALL-mode (BACKFILL-inclusive, dispatched for "statistical power") and REAL_ONLY mode materially DISAGREE on the velocity axis specifically — ALL-mode "Fast" is deeply negative at short horizons before turning barely positive at 240-bar, the opposite shape from REAL_ONLY's clean monotonic growth. Pooling BACKFILL for power is not a safe proxy here — the synthetic and real populations measure different underlying distributions, not just different N of the same one.

Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM post_t1_continuation_forward_return_premise_check` (PROVISIONAL, per the sourcing decision's own instruction to record regardless of outcome) and updated `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days` with the concrete next step: run `computeRigor()`/chronological-stability + replication (`server/services/rigorDiagnostics.js`) specifically on the velocity=Fast real-only slice before trusting it. If it survives, it's the lead candidate for DeepSeek's Alternative B (clock/velocity-based hold, tested against a time-matched blind control). If it doesn't: every axis proposed across 7 rounds (day_type/BALANCE, setup_type, time_of_day, A-level, velocity) will have been tested and found negative, an artifact, or unusable — the "let winners run" thread would then be genuinely exhausted, not just under-tested, and should close with no mechanism built.

## ✅ 2026-08-16 (3rd follow-up session): runner-trail thread reaches a real, decisive answer for BALANCE — 2 rounds of DeepSeek design critique, a real direction-inference bug found and fixed, TREND/TURBULENT correctly left open

Continued the runner-trail thread again. `docs/RUNNER_TRAIL_REALDATA_SPEC.md` (built, never run) and `server/services/runnerTrailSim.js` (built, never run) were sent to DeepSeek for a Phase-0 design critique before building the spec's backtest script. **Verdict: do not build it as written.** Two blocking bugs confirmed against the actual code: (1) `replayBarsWithAtrTrail`/`replayBarsWithStructuralTrail` accept a `t1` parameter and never reference it — the mechanism silently deletes the T1 profit-take entirely, and combined with this population's median 0.67 reward:risk, the grid's `activationR=1.0` floor already sits above where most winners bank; (2) the spec's day_type "no-lookahead fix" doesn't fix anything, since `day_type` is an EOD (20:20 ET) label, not knowable at 10:30 as assumed — already-established fact from an earlier session, re-confirmed. DeepSeek's alternative: parameterize the LIVE `stepBreakevenTrail()` (keeps T1, non-tautological, snaps to breakeven) with a per-trade `atrMult × ATR` width instead of a stored calibration row — and pointed out `scratch/cheap_test_v2.mjs` already half-builds this from the prior session.

Re-ran `cheap_test_v2.mjs` for real numbers: negative everywhere at the one width tested (`atrMult=2.0`) — BALANCE Δ-4.45, TREND Δ-2.88, TURBULENT Δ-5.09 — and found TREND/TURBULENT are severely day-clustered (6 and 3 distinct real trading days respectively, 95.5%/100% top5Share) in a way DeepSeek's spec-only review hadn't seen. Sent this back for a second-round critique on whether to sweep further; DeepSeek predicted the `2.0` cell was likely saturated (stop pinned at breakeven, never lifting) given BALANCE's mean-reverting excursions. Built a cheap causal diagnostic (`scratch/breakeven_trail_liftoff_diagnostic.mjs`, walks the real `stepBreakevenTrail()` step function bar-by-bar) to check before committing to a full 6-cell sweep — first attempt used a lookahead global-peak proxy (92.5% lift-off, caught as too-clean-to-trust and fixed to the causal version: 60.1%). That **contradicted** DeepSeek's saturation prediction. Sent back a third time; DeepSeek conceded the saturation guess was wrong, found a real bug in the process (direction-inference defaults diverged between `cheap_test_v2.mjs` and the new diagnostic for directionless setup_types like `ZONE_EDGE_FADE`, verified real — 13 such trades exist in the population), and reconciled the corrected 60-61% lift-off rate as consistent with (not contradicting) a genuine BALANCE negative: lift-off is necessary but not sufficient for "beats breakeven," since the mechanism's real cost is the breakeven-snap-on-arm itself giving back the T1 profit on any trade that touches T1 and reverts (BALANCE's default behavior).

Fixed the direction bug in both scripts (`long = t1 > entry`, correct for every setup_type including contextual-direction ones, no name-parsing) — verified the fix left `cheap_test_v2.mjs`'s headline numbers unchanged (its old default happened to already match all 13 real `ZONE_EDGE_FADE` trades in this population by coincidence) while the diagnostic's own count shifted modestly (60.1%→61.2%), confirming the finding is robust to the fix. Added median-delta and %-beats-baseline reporting to `cheap_test_v2.mjs` per DeepSeek's "decisive" caveat: **BALANCE median delta is exactly $0.00, only 14.3% of trades ever beat baseline** — not a few large losers dragging a mean, a mechanism that rarely helps and usually gives back profit when it does. Recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM breakeven_trail_2xatr_balance_negative` (N=540, 18 real days, 47.4% top5Share — a legitimately decisive read). TREND/TURBULENT were measured but deliberately **not** claimed — too day-clustered to mean anything yet; flagged `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days` (MEDIUM) to revisit once real N accumulates more distinct trading days, using the same rigor bar that made the BALANCE read trustworthy.

**Net state of the original question** ("let winners run without diluting BALANCE"): BALANCE — answered, no, don't trail at this width. TREND/TURBULENT — the actual original hypothesis (trail where the fixed exit is already weak) — still genuinely unknown, not negative, just underpowered. `docs/RUNNER_TRAIL_REALDATA_SPEC.md` and `server/services/runnerTrailSim.js` remain unbuilt-on/unused (their T1-deletion design flaw makes them unsuitable regardless of data) — still on the file-naming cleanup list flagged earlier this session (`docs/backtest_mnq_20260814.py` etc.), not yet acted on.

**6th round, same day — Gemini AND DeepSeek independently converged on the same correction to the plan, without seeing each other's response. START HERE if picking this thread back up.** Full detail on `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days` (updated, self-contained). Short version: **don't wait for TREND/TURBULENT to hit 15 real days before doing anything.** Run `scripts/backtest_post_resolution_sequence.mjs`'s forward-return check (CLAUDE.md item 4a's own convention) TODAY on the full historical population — it's a retrospective premise check, not a live decision, so the day-count floor doesn't block it. Slice by **setup_type and time-of-day**, not `day_type` (both reviewers independently rejected day_type as the primary axis — N=6/N=3 real days means one outlier day skews the whole bucket, and DeepSeek's sharper point: even a perfect day_type-conditioned backtest answer could never ship as a live day-type-gated mechanism anyway, since no safe live "today is TREND" signal exists). Gemini added two new, verified-live-computable candidate cuts neither Claude nor DeepSeek had raised: **trade velocity** (time from entry to T1 — fast may mean impulsive/trending, slow may mean BALANCE-chop) and **`a_up_fired`/`a_down_fired`** (confirmed via `server/index.js`'s `autoComputeTodayACD()` to update live during the session, unlike `day_type` — a trade taken WITH the day's A-level break has real directional alignment; one FADING an A-level is pure mean-reversion and probably shouldn't be trailed at all). If the forward-return check comes back near-zero/negative everywhere: the whole "let winners run" thread closes permanently, no exit mechanism needed. If positive for specific setup_types/cuts: those become the candidates for the mechanism ranking already recorded (time-hold, structural stop, order-flow-gated trail).

**5th round, same day — user caught Claude generating zero independent ideas all session, then DeepSeek's review of Claude's 3 self-generated alternatives surfaced the actual highest-leverage reframing of the whole thread.** User: "Your not providing any alternative ways to look at this but deepseek is." Correct — every substantive idea across rounds 1-4 originated from DeepSeek; Claude's own contributions were all auditing. Claude proposed 3 alternatives (scale-out, gate the trail on a live continuation signal, static wider target for the fat-tail subset) and asked DeepSeek to critique them AND generate its own. Self-correction caught before dispatch: scale-out is fully DEAD, not fresh — `docs/SCALEOUT_RUNNER_SPEC.md` shows a fractional scale-out design was built and invalidated the SAME DAY (2026-07-19) by a real 1-contract execution constraint; the current live `stepBreakevenTrail()` mechanism (tested all night) is literally what that spec was revised INTO.

DeepSeek's review, verified claim-by-claim against the repo:
- **#1 (scale-out): dead, confirmed.** The "two separate 1-contract legs" workaround is real but is a sizing/portfolio question, not an exit mechanism, and it re-opens the exact stacking risk the 1-contract constraint exists to prevent.
- **#2 (gate on a continuation signal): real shape, wrong implementation.** Gating arming (not just width) on an independent condition is a genuine mechanism change, but it relocates rather than escapes the tautology trap this codebase's confound checklist already warns about — needs a time-matched blind-gate control. Worse: `bar6_checkpoint` (one of the two signals proposed) is the WRONG tool — verified against `maeMfeReplay.js`, its validated direction is "exit early" (RECOVERING + near target → take profit now), not "hold longer" — pointing it at "hold longer" inverts its own validated meaning. `STACK_VOL_BREAK_LIVE` is the right *kind* of signal (order-flow, not price-path) but is a breakout signal being proposed for fade setups (opposite direction) and is still PROVISIONAL.
- **#3 (static wider target): correct observation, dead dataset.** N=8 real trades for the fat-tail subset (already known, per `cheap_test_v2.mjs`'s own comment), AND the entire `WIDER_TARGET_EV_TEST` family is separately flagged contaminated by a confirmed `mfe_points` blind spot (`OPEN_DECISION optimal_target_blind_to_post_resolution_continuation`, HIGH) — reviving it would be re-mining a known-bad measurement.

**The reframing that actually matters, verified against 3 independent sources in the repo**: the TREND/TURBULENT split this whole thread has been chasing is a BACKTEST STRATIFICATION, not a live-actionable gate — `day_type` is an EOD (20:20 ET) label, structurally null the entire live session (confirmed: `test_invariants.mjs`'s own invariant check). Worse, the one intraday proxy already tried for a related purpose (wide IB range predicting a "big day") was found to predict the WRONG bucket — top-decile-IB days are 52.8% TURBULENT vs a 17.3% base rate, only 16.7% TREND (below the 21.6% base rate) — `RESEARCH_CLAIM intraday_ib_range_daytype_conditioning`, verified exact numbers. And the one live day-type estimate actually tried as a real live gate input was found NET HARMFUL — `OPEN_DECISION dtclass_null_all_day_neuters_multiple_live_gates` (RESOLVED, N=440, net delta -$3,238.60, 70.6% false-positive rate), verified exact numbers. This means even once TREND/TURBULENT clear the ≥15-real-days floor for a trustworthy backtest, there is currently NO safe live signal for "today is a TREND day" — any mechanism built on this split needs its own separate live-proxy validation before it can ever ship.

**DeepSeek's own 4 alternatives, ranked, full detail on `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days` (updated with the complete reframing so it's self-contained after a context clear)**: (1) **Alternative C, highest priority** — before building any exit mechanism, run this codebase's own cheapest/most-decisive test (the stop-free conditional-vs-unconditional forward-return check) on TREND/TURBULENT specifically: is there any continuation to capture post-T1 at all? If no, the runner question closes without another mechanism built. (2) **Alternative B** — if C shows time-structured continuation (matches an existing domain claim on SHORT-vs-LONG resolution timing), test a clock-based hold instead of a price trail — different giveback shape, won't be shaken out by intra-window whipsaw. (3) **Alternative A** — if the real problem is runners never surviving TO T1 (stopped out on retracement), a structural/level-informed stop relocation via the already-built-but-unused `computeStructuralStopAnchors()` (`runnerTrailSim.js`) + `pilot_structural_stop_placement.mjs`, control-gated against a `FIXED_AT_MEDIAN` baseline. (4) **Alternative D** (the salvageable core of Claude's #2) — gate trail width (not binary arming) on order-flow confirmation, direction-split by fade side, tested against a time-matched blind-gate control — lowest priority since it stacks two uncertainties (a PROVISIONAL signal × a thin direction split).

Saved as persistent feedback (`feedback_delegate_verify_loop_no_independent_ideas.md`) so future sessions bring independent framings alongside dispatching to Gemini/DeepSeek, not only after being asked.

**4th round, same day — DeepSeek caught real overreach in the recorded claim, then a shell-interpolation bug corrupted the first correction attempt, caught and fixed same-turn.** Sent the recorded `RESEARCH_CLAIM` back for critique before trusting it (user's explicit instruction, not the default flow). DeepSeek found the population-wide median ($0.00) and %-beats-baseline (14.3%) were misleading: any trade that never reaches T1 takes a byte-identical path in both the baseline and the trail arm by construction, so those stats are dominated by the never-armed mass and say nothing about trail quality once it actually engages — the claim's central sentence ("when the trail changes the outcome it's usually worse") was only supported by the mean, not the stats printed. Fixed by instrumenting `simulateBreakevenTrail()` to return an `armed` flag and adding armed-conditional stats (mean/median/%beat/%lose) plus day-level aggregation (mean-of-per-day-means, count of negative days) to `cheap_test_v2.mjs`. Corrected BALANCE numbers, conditional on the trail actually arming (N=299, 55.4% armed): mean Δ-$8.04/trade, median Δ-$40.00/trade, only 25.8% beat baseline, 74.2% lose; day-level: 11 of 18 real days net-negative. A materially stronger, better-supported result than the original. **Separately caught while re-recording the correction**: a `node -e "..."` shell call with the corrected claim text in double quotes let bash interpolate literal `$` dollar-amounts as shell variables (`$8.04`→empty+`.04`, `$0.19`→`/bin/bash`+`.19` via `$0`), silently writing corrupted numbers into `performance_audit`. Caught by verifying the write same-turn (per the standing verify-writes rule) rather than assuming success from the "recorded" console log — re-wrote via a proper `.mjs` file (no shell interpolation) and re-verified clean. Final corrected claim: `sampleSize=299` (armed subset, matching the headline `ev_per_trade=-8.04`), `RESEARCH_CLAIM breakeven_trail_2xatr_balance_negative` now explicitly states it does not bear on TREND/TURBULENT and cross-references `OPEN_DECISION runner_trail_trend_turbulent_needs_more_days`, which was also updated with a concrete numeric unblock threshold (TREND and TURBULENT each need ≥15 distinct real trading days) stated in prose, since `flagDecision()` has no structured `unblockCondition` field the way `recordClaim()` does.

## 🟡 2026-08-16 (follow-up session): breakeven-trail correctness gap root-caused and fixed — a second, forgotten insert path, not the lookup logic itself; real data too thin to calibrate any of the 6 variants right now

Picked up exactly where the runner-trail thread below stopped: `docs/OPEN_THREADS.md`'s own "next session should start with" instructions. Re-ran `scripts/backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs` fresh — **zero of the 6 live-wired `_TRAIL` variants survive the funnel today** (Tier A and Tier B both 0/7 survivors). Root cause is real data thinness, not a script bug: pooled real (`origin_status IN ('ACTIVE','SHADOW')`) `TARGET_HIT` counts for the 6 base types range 0-13, all below `MIN_N=20` — confirmed directly against `active_setups` (most of each type's history is `BACKFILL`; e.g. `PW_HIGH_FADE_LONG_TRAIL` has zero rows ever). The one surviving calibration row, `B_FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG` (trail=20.3pt), only still exists because the script's protective DELETE never wipes a row a live `CONDITIONAL_VARIANTS` entry reads — but it's stale from 2026-08-03, computed **before** the 2026-08-10 origin_status real-data-only filter was added to this script, so even that one row was built mostly on synthetic data. Flagged `OPEN_DECISION breakeven_trail_zero_real_survivors_20260816` (MEDIUM) for whether to null it out proactively (matching the `OPTIMAL_STOP` circuit-breaker precedent) or let it ride since it's SHADOW-only/no capital risk.

**The real correctness bug, found and fixed same session.** All 14 real `_TRAIL` fires had `runner_trail_width=NULL` despite `acd.js`'s main candidate INSERT (~line 8440) having a correct width lookup — because that lookup was dead code for these types. Every one of the 6 `_TRAIL` types is permanently `THIN_N` in `SETUP_STATUS` (by construction: `resolveSetupType()` unconditionally diverts every touch to the `_TRAIL` name, so the base type can never accumulate a fresh real trade, and `liveStats._suppressedSetups` treats `THIN_N` the same as `SUPPRESS`) — so every real touch actually flows through a **different, earlier insert path**, the "suppressed near-level audit" branch (~line 7114-7194), which was written before the trail mechanism existed and never had a `runner_trail_width` column or lookup at all. Fixed by mirroring the exact same `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` lookup into that branch (`server/routes/acd.js`). Verified via `node --check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build` (all clean); `test_invariants.mjs`'s 8 pre-existing FAILs (5 missing-calibration-row + 3 unrelated `OPTIMAL_STOP` circuit-breaker trips) confirmed identical via `git stash` before/after — none introduced by this fix. **Not yet observed on a real live fire** — market was closed the entire session (Sunday, before 6PM ET Globex reopen). Resolves `OPEN_DECISION breakeven_trail_never_engaged_5of6_rows_missing`.

**Still open, per the original thread's own estimate**: the Arm-A-minus-Arm-B (T1-floor vs breakeven-snap) trail-vs-fixed comparison DeepSeek scoped cannot proceed yet — there's no real, uncontaminated calibrated width to test against. Next step is letting real `SHADOW` data accumulate under the now-fixed insert path (future real touches will finally populate `runner_trail_width` once a variant's own calibration exists) and revisiting once at least one variant clears `MIN_N=20` on real-only data.

## 🔴 2026-08-16: "Let winners run" runner-trail thread — 2 real bugs fixed, 2 structural framing errors corrected, 1 tautology debunked, and a real live-mechanism correctness gap found — stopped deliberately mid-thread, not finished

User asked to explore letting winners run further (a trailing exit past the current fixed target) while preserving the fade roster's real edge on BALANCE days. Two unrelated crypto-exchange scripts (`scripts/tripwire_backtest_framework.py`/`numba_tripwire_backtest.py`, confirmed by both DeepSeek and Gemini to be copied from an unrelated project) were deleted at the start of this thread — not part of the narrative below.

**Bug 1 — `computeCorrectedTarget()` NaN, real and now fixed.** Its mark-to-market branch for unresolved trades read `lastBar.close`, but the bar-loading query in 4 callers never selected `close` — every unresolved trade computed NaN, poisoning the aggregate EV sum for any candidate with ≥1 unresolved trade. This silently blocked `IB_BEARISH`/`IB_BULLISH`/`PD_POC_FADE_SHORT` from ever being evaluated for a corrected target. Fixed in `update_optimal_stops.mjs`, `backtest_target_sweep_v2.mjs`, `backtest_ib_daytype_stop_target.mjs`, `diagnose_chronological_stop_target_bias.mjs`; verified live (both IB types now correctly *fail* their OOS-beats-baseline guardrail with real numbers instead of never being attempted). Resolves `OPEN_DECISION target_correction_oos_nan_bug_3_setup_types`. Committed live: `update_optimal_stops.mjs` re-run (117 rows) + `backtest_ib_daytype_stop_target.mjs` re-run (4 day-type rows, real find: `IB_BEARISH_TREND` ev=+$16.04/trade N=84).

**Bug 2 — `computeMaeMfe()` naive-ET-timestamp round-trip corruption, real and now fixed.** Binding a fetched `fired_at` (naive `timestamp without time zone`, no tz) back into a new query as a JS Date parameter round-trips through Postgres's real UTC serialization + the session's `America/New_York` timezone, silently shifting it 4-5 hours (`10:30:00` reproduced as `05:30:00`). Confirmed it pulled hours of unrelated overnight price action into what should have been a same-morning bar window. Fixed by re-serializing to a naive string before binding. Never ran live — its only caller (`scripts/audit_worst_trades_maemfe.mjs`, a legitimate 2026-08-12 script that had sat uncommitted and unrun) is now safe and committed.

**Framing error 1 (DeepSeek design critique):** the original spec (`docs/RUNNER_TRAIL_REALDATA_SPEC.md`) gated the trail on `acd_daily_log.day_type` (BALANCE/TREND/TURBULENT), assumed knowable at IB close ~10:30 ET. Wrong — it's an EOD ground-truth label, not written until `derive_day_types.js`'s 20:20 ET cron. Matches the already-open `dtclass_null_all_day_neuters_multiple_live_gates` decision — confirmed live that the existing `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` day-type sizing mechanism has this identical defect (`dtClass` reads null essentially the whole session). A broader DeepSeek regime-strategy critique found this codebase has ~13 regime-adjacent mechanisms and only one (`volatilityRegimeService.js`'s DIRECTIONAL/CHOP classifier) is validated+live+consumed; recommended treating day-type gating as an explicit "perfect-foresight ceiling," never a deployable gate.

**Framing error 2:** a cheap test splitting trail-vs-fixed EV delta on the validated DIRECTIONAL/CHOP classifier came back uniformly negative — but DeepSeek's review found 3 more real bugs (TIME_EXPIRED trades dropped instead of marked-to-market, biasing the trail arm down; `activationR=2.0` wasn't neutral — median reward:risk in this population is 0.67, so it silently deleted T1 entirely; the regime JOIN double-counted ~37 trades via an undeduped `VOL_REGIME_HIST` read) and that the DIRECTIONAL/CHOP split itself was untestable — only 1 HIGH-VOL-DIRECTIONAL day exists in the whole window.

**Tautology found:** a corrected v2 test (fixed accounting, split on `day_type` instead, two conservative mechanism shapes) showed a "BALANCE +$8.47/trade" result for a "bank-at-T1-then-trail" arm — DeepSeek proved this is a **weak-dominance tautology, not a finding**: that arm's PnL is mathematically guaranteed ≥ baseline on every single trade (floors at T1, never below), so a positive aggregate delta only measures how often price ran past T1 on BALANCE days, not whether trailing adds value. The only matched-risk arm tested (the real live `stepBreakevenTrail()`, which snaps to breakeven not T1 and so can genuinely lose) was negative everywhere, but used an uncalibrated trail width.

**Real correctness gap found while checking whether to proceed (DeepSeek, live DB queries):** the live breakeven-then-trail mechanism (6 `_TRAIL` setup_types, SHADOW-only, no capital risk) has **never actually engaged the trail on a single real trade** — all 14 real SHADOW rows show `runner_trail_width=NULL`, and 5 of 6 `BREAKEVEN_TRAIL_TEST` calibration rows are missing from `performance_audit` (matching `INVARIANT_FAIL`s unfixed since 2026-08-03). Even the one surviving calibration row (`FLOOR_S1_FADE_LONG`, since 2026-07-19) never got its width applied to its 2 real trades. Flagged `OPEN_DECISION breakeven_trail_never_engaged_5of6_rows_missing` (HIGH) — confirms and sharpens the already-tracked `acd_trail_null_fallback_silent` decision with concrete live evidence.

**Deliberately stopped here, not finished — DeepSeek's own recommendation.** The well-specified next step (reframe as Arm-A-minus-Arm-B — T1-floor vs breakeven-snap, a genuine non-tautological comparison — using the *real* calibrated `runner_trail_width` for the 6 live `_TRAIL` variants instead of a guessed width) is NOT executable until the correctness gap above is fixed (there's no width to pull for 5/6 variants, and the 6th's width was never applied). DeepSeek's estimate if resuming: 30-90min to reconcile the missing calibration rows + 15-45min to fix the insert-time width lookup + 2-4hrs to build the Arm-A-minus-Arm-B comparison correctly — realistically half a day, not a quick follow-up. **Next session should start with**: re-run `scripts/backtest_breakeven_trail.mjs`, reconcile why 5/6 `trailSignalName`s produce no row, fix the width-application bug, and only then decide whether to revisit the trail-vs-fixed comparison — on data that's actually testing what it claims to.

`server/services/runnerTrailSim.js` (causal ATR-band + structural-zigzag trail replay functions) was built and committed as research infrastructure, not wired live. `scratch/cheap_test_v2.mjs` and predecessors are gitignored scratch files, not preserved — the methodology and result are fully captured here and in the flagged decision.

## 🟡 2026-08-16: Promotion-pipeline structural fix built — DeepSeek QA caught 2 real bugs before ship, both fixed same session; partial close, not the clean "3 stuck types fixed" it first looked like

Fresh session after a context clear, picked up `docs/PROMOTION_PIPELINE_STRUCTURAL_FIX_SPEC.md` exactly as it was written to be picked up — no re-derivation needed. Built all 3 locked-order layers: (1) `test_invariants.mjs` check `[20]`, a standing reachability invariant (any `SETUP_STATUS`-`ACTIVE`/`PROMOTE` type 30+ days old with zero real `origin_status='ACTIVE'` rows ever gets WARNed); (2) `server/services/setupEligibility.js` (`computeSuppressionSets`/`isLiveEligible`), extracted verbatim from `acd.js`'s existing suppression-set construction and wired into `shadowCandidates`' INSERT (the F3 fix — this array used to hardcode `'SHADOW','SHADOW'` for ~20 event setup types with no promotion path at all, despite a code comment claiming one existed); (3) migrated the 3 standalone-poller promotion gates off their own hand-rolled `N>=20/EV<-$5` reimplementation, which all 3 still excluded `TIME_EXPIRED`-resolved trades from (a bug already fixed in `backtest_setup_status.mjs` on 2026-08-03, never propagated).

**User explicitly asked for DeepSeek to QA the issue and the fix before calling it done.** That QA (dispatched via the standard `scripts/invoke_deepseek.sh` protocol, request self-contained with the full diff) found 9 findings; Claude independently verified the load-bearing ones before acting (per the standing "audit all model output" rule) rather than trusting the writeup — every verified claim held:

1. **HIGH, confirmed by direct code read — a real regression, not a style nit.** `scripts/backtest_momentum60_daytype.mjs` writes the `SETUP_STATUS` row for `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` from `simulate(trendEvts, ...)` — a bar-history backtest over `price_bars_primary`, never from real `active_setups` trades (`backtest_setup_status.mjs`, the real-forward writer, only writes a row for a `setup_type` that already has `active_setups` rows, and this family has had zero, ever). Layer 3's first-draft migration would have pointed `minuteBarSignalDetector.js`'s `getLiveStatus()` at that backtest-derived row via the new `getCanonicalLiveStatus()`, firing the detector `origin_status='ACTIVE'` on its first-ever real touch with **zero** real forward validation — the precise opposite of what Layer 3 exists to do. **Reverted**: kept the original hand-rolled query, added `TIME_EXPIRED` to its resolution filter (the one real, narrow gap Layer 3 was actually about for this family) — the "shadow until N≥20 real trades" discipline is intact.
2. **HIGH, confirmed by re-reading my own code — a structural fail-open hole in the new gate itself.** `isLiveEligible()` inferred eligibility purely from *absence* from the suppressed set — meaning a setup_type with **no** `SETUP_STATUS` row at all (never calibrated, or hit mid-request during a transient query failure) read as eligible, not blocked. This is the exact "fundamental" failure shape the user raised, just relocated into the fix meant to close it. Fixed: `computeSuppressionSets` now also returns a `knownTypes` Set; `isLiveEligible` requires `knownTypes.has(setupType)` before it can return true — a setup_type without a row now defaults to SHADOW, matching `getCanonicalLiveStatus`'s existing fail-closed "no row → SHADOW" behavior instead of disagreeing with it.
3. **Confirmed by re-reading check `[20]`'s own logic.** Its exact-string match (`everFiredSet.has(r.signal_name)`) can never clear for a *pooled* family name (SETUP_STATUS calibrates `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` with no suffix; real fires would land under `_LONG`/`_SHORT`) — a permanent false positive even once the fix genuinely works. Fixed generically (no hand-maintained family list, matching the check's own design philosophy): also treat a signal_name as "fired" if any real fired `setup_type` starts with `${signal_name}_`.
4. **Availability regression, confirmed by re-reading my own diff.** `computeSuppressionSets`'s two queries were missing the `.catch(() => ({rows:[]}))` the original inline queries had — a transient DB error would now throw all the way up through the outer `try/catch` and 500 the *entire* `setup-detection` response (not just skip suppression), where the old code degraded silently instead. Re-added the `.catch()`, paired with the Finding-2 fix so the caught-error case (`knownTypes` empty) still fails *closed* on suppression rather than reverting to the old fail-open state.
5. **Stale comment, confirmed against `docs/DECISIONS_LOG.md:319`.** The `STOP_SWEEP_PAUSED` exclusion comment said the pause decision was "still unresolved" — it was actually resolved to PAUSED on 2026-08-05 (both LONG and SHORT). Fixed the comment; the exclusion itself was already behaviorally correct.
6. Closure-capture safety of the fire-and-forget `shadowCandidates` INSERT IIFE reading `liveStats._suppressedSetups`/`_dowSuppressToday`: **verified safe** (there are two `liveStats` bindings in the file — a block-scoped `let` at line 6134 used by the earlier level-fade logic, and a separate outer `const liveStats = getCached(...)` at line 7664 that the IIFE actually closes over; `setCached` always runs before 7664 in the same request, and the Sets are written once per day, never mutated). Added the `?.` optional-chaining convention every other consumer in the file uses, as defense against a future refactor moving the `setCached` call — not because a real bug existed today.

**What's genuinely NOT fixed by this build**, flagged rather than silently left implied: of the 3 setup_types check `[20]` originally found stuck, only `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` was addressed (via the narrower `TIME_EXPIRED` fix, not a promotion-path change). `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_BALANCE_FADE` (no detector fires it at all) and `WPP_FADE_SHORT_GAP_UP` (still hardcoded `'SHADOW','SHADOW'` at `acd.js` ~7164 and ~8139 — different insert paths than the `shadowCandidates` array F3 fixed) are untouched — and DeepSeek confirmed both of *their* `SETUP_STATUS` rows are also backtest-derived, so they'd hit the exact same Finding-1 trap if wired the same way. Split into `OPEN_DECISION promotion_pipeline_remaining_stuck_types` (MEDIUM). A lower-stakes leftover — ~10 display/stats (not live-promotion-gate) `TIME_EXPIRED`-exclusion occurrences from the original 2026-08-03 triage list, across `acd.js`/`setups.js`/`stats.js`/`monteCarloService.js`/`patternMemoryUpdate.js` — split into `OPEN_DECISION time_expired_display_stats_sweep_remaining` (LOW).

**Resolved**: `shadowcandidates_hardcoded_no_promotion_path` (F3 shipped, verified via a Set-equality diff against live data plus direct `isLiveEligible()` sanity checks) and `time_expired_exclusion_pattern_broader_audit`'s live-promotion-gate scope (all 3 gates now correctly include `TIME_EXPIRED`, no `origin_status='ACTIVE'` count regression). `promotion_pipeline_structural_fix_2026_08_16` itself stays `PENDING` (HIGH) — updated with the honest partial status above, not resolved, per DeepSeek's own explicit recommendation not to record this as a clean close.

**Not yet verified — the market was closed the entire build** (Sunday, before the 6PM ET Globex reopen). Lint/syntax/Set-equality/sanity-check verification is as strong as it gets without a real poll cycle; the actual proof (`test_invariants.mjs` check `[20]`'s WARN list shrinking as real `ACTIVE` fires accumulate through the fixed `shadowCandidates` path) still needs the market to run for a while. Next session/check-in: re-run check `[20]` and look for `MOMENTUM_60m_60m_TREND` or any `shadowCandidates`-family type dropping off the list.

## ✅ 2026-08-12: Defended-level-retest entry idea — scoped, DeepSeek-critiqued, mined, resolved NEGATIVE, all same session

User observation from a real chart: comparing a losing `OR5_LOW_FADE_SHORT` (09:45, sliced through with no confirmation) against a winning one (10:59, a real sequence of failed bounce attempts before reversing). Refined over several rounds into a precise, testable hypothesis — a level fade should require a retest that *stalls* (>=2 failed bounce/reclaim attempts, weakening), not just any touch, symmetric for longs and shorts. DeepSeek design critique (before any mining) caught two real, load-bearing problems in the first-pass definition: the "weakening delta" claim was contradicted by the user's own annotated example under the natural aggregation, and the "multiple failed attempts" framing relocated rather than avoided the exact tautology trap that killed `intrabar_cvd_divergence_no_edge_confounded` on 2026-07-21. Fixed by testing two separate, non-tautological weakening metrics in parallel (bounce price-extent vs. failure-bar delta magnitude) and expanding to a 5-population/4-comparison-pair design with a genuine entry-timing-matched control, not just a blind-delay one.

**Result, from a real 8-way window/variant sweep with a proper `computeReplication()` check**: negative in all 8 combinations, never beats blind immediate entry in any of them, no robust gain against the timing-matched control once entry-delay is isolated. The dominant effect: waiting *at all* — regardless of pattern — underperforms blind entry, closely matching `engagement_confirmation_entry_timing`'s prior finding on a related idea. `RESEARCH_CLAIM defended_level_retest_confirmation_entry_negative`; resolves `OPEN_DECISION wait_for_held_ground_confirmation_before_fade_entry`. Full design + result: `docs/DEFENDED_LEVEL_RETEST_SPEC.md`.

**Three real mining bugs caught by direct code/script audit, not by trusting Gemini's prose summaries** — worth recording since each would have produced a wrong or overstated finding if accepted at face value: (1) a false "touch-detection isn't importable" blocker on the first pass (it already was, in `scripts/backtest_unified.js`); (2) a hardcoded-Monday-stop/target bug plus a coverage-cost metric that was never actually computed despite being reported as if it were, on the second pass; (3) a `computeReplication()` call with a broken/incomplete args object, silently returning degenerate zeros while the write-up described it as a real (if unfavorable) result. Built the actual 8-way sweep directly rather than a third Gemini round, reusing the already-verified simulation engine. Consolidating the mining script into a real, promoted `scripts/backtest_defended_level_retest.mjs` (importing the real shared functions instead of a duplicated copy, per a file-size nudge) surfaced a fourth bug — the import silently dropped fields the second-pass fix had added, zeroing out most of the populations' P&L — caught by re-verifying byte-identical output before trusting the refactor.

## ✅ 2026-08-12: "Why did OR5_MID only fire once despite several touches?" — root-caused and fixed same-session; the first two conclusions along the way were both wrong, corrected before shipping

**Superseded, don't rebuild on the version below** — kept only for the corrective narrative. Original investigation concluded `cascadeBreaker` (a >=3-distinct-stop/45min circuit breaker) was legitimately suppressing OR5_MID_FADE_SHORT's touches, and even quantified its aggregate EV (`RESEARCH_CLAIM cascade_breaker_suppressed_ev_unstable_recent_reversal`, `OPEN_DECISION cascade_breaker_recency_reversal_needs_reassessment`). **Both wrong.** A DeepSeek design critique (dispatched for an unrelated redesign proposal) caught that `cascadeBreaker` was already disabled as an acting gate on 2026-08-05 (user-authorized, after an earlier validation found it blocked outperforming trades on 5 of 6 test days) — it has only logged an audit trail since then, never actually blocked anything. The "hand-verified" active-each-time check was real, but verified the wrong thing (that the flag was on, not that the flag caused anything — the exact frozen-anchor/correlation-as-causation trap this codebase's own confound checklist warns about, applied to this investigation itself). Both the `RESEARCH_CLAIM` and `OPEN_DECISION` above were corrected/superseded same-session.

**The real finding — ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED same-session, commit `7b4ebca`.** With the cascade breaker's role eliminated, why did the REAL candidate-detection pipeline (not the vestigial audit branch) produce **zero rows of any kind** during cascade-flagged windows? Confirmed across 8+ different setup_types over a 42-minute stretch (09:50-10:32 ET) — not just OR5_MID, also `RTH_VWAP_FADE_LONG` (a cleared, real +$6.66/trade edge, shut out of 5 of 8 touches that day). Diagnostic logging shipped mid-investigation (3 checkpoints, gated on `cascadeBreaker.active`, persisted to `scratch/cascade_diag.log`) caught it live within minutes: `nearLevels.reduce()` picked a single "primary" candidate per poll by **pooled** (long+short) EV with **no fallback** if that pick was excluded by any suppression check — `WEEKLY_OPEN_FADE` (`THIN_N`, cluster-blocked) kept winning the pooled-EV pick every single poll for 4+ minutes while `RTH_VWAP_FADE_LONG` sat unevaluated in the same cluster the whole time. Quantified impact for the day: 66 orphaned-candidate instances across 30 distinct setup_types (visible only during cascade-active windows, via the audit branch — very likely a floor, not the true blast radius, since the same flaw would bite on any clustered-levels poll regardless of cascade involvement), 4 of those 30 real `ACTIVE` setups, `RTH_VWAP_FADE_LONG` never firing live once that day because of it.

Fixed: candidates are now sorted by **directional** EV (not pooled — a real bug DeepSeek's design critique caught in my own first sketch) and filtered to the approach-consistent side of price, then tried in EV order until one clears every existing suppression gate — nothing bypasses any existing calibration/quality bar, this only changes which candidate gets a chance to clear it. 2-round DeepSeek review (design critique before code, code review after) — the review round traced every downstream variable reference across the full 10,985-line file and found nothing further; it did catch that my own first-draft "re-derive cleared from the suppression flags" approach would have let a wrong-side-filtered candidate silently fire anyway, fixed with an explicit `winnerFound` flag before shipping. A separate transient `ReferenceError` hit during the edit sequence was traced to a mid-edit nodemon reload serving a syntactically-incomplete intermediate file, not a real bug — confirmed via DeepSeek's independent scope hunt plus clean live requests post-restart. `OPEN_DECISION cascade_diag_awaiting_live_data` now RESOLVED with the full writeup; `OPEN_DECISION remove_cascade_diag_after_confirmed` (LOW) tracks removing the temporary diagnostic logging once a few more live occurrences confirm the fallback's selection quality (not yet observed: a case where it actually rescues a real 2nd/3rd-best candidate, only correctly-empty no-fallback-available cases so far). The misleading "FADE REGIME OFF" frontend banner (still shows when `cascadeBreaker.active` is true even though nothing is blocked) was found in the same investigation and remains unfixed, separate work.

Along the way, also surfaced with real bar-level evidence (not just chart-reading): the user's separate observation that a losing `OR5_LOW_FADE_SHORT` entry (09:45, sliced straight through the level) and a winning one (10:59, price probed, rallied back, stalled on weakening volume, then reversed) differ in a real, checkable way — not "was there a pause" but "what did volume/delta do during it." This matches an already-flagged-but-unbuilt idea, `OPEN_DECISION wait_for_held_ground_confirmation_before_fade_entry` (2026-07-27) — not a rehash of the candle-pattern/CVD-divergence ideas already tested negative in `docs/CANDLE_ORDERFLOW_RESEARCH_SPEC.md`. Known trap already on record if this gets built: `engagement_confirmation_entry_timing` found a similar wait-for-confirmation idea's apparent edge was fully explained by the delay itself, not the confirmation logic — any real test needs a blind-delay control. A third, apparently new thread also surfaced and is unbuilt: whether stop placement should account for nearby structural levels (a fixed-point stop from the *first* touched level in a cluster may land closer to a *different* nearby level's liquidity than a stop from a level further back would) — no existing `OPEN_DECISION` covers this yet.

Also surfaced in passing, not yet investigated: `OR5_LOW_FADE_LONG` is `SUPPRESS` despite a *positive* blended EV (+$1.12/trade, N=340) — doesn't fit the documented "N≥20, EV<-$5" auto-suppress rule at face value; worth a look next time this thread is picked up.

## 🟡 2026-08-12: "Is the live system doing well because of the type of day, or is something improving?" — real answer: neither pattern the user expected, and one real gap found

User asked why the live system was +$1,400 on a quiet/BALANCE-leaning day, then asked the bigger question: what do good vs. bad days look like historically, and how do we manage the bad ones to keep large losses down. Checked real numbers rather than guessing (full detail in the two DB rows below, not repeated here):

- The apparent "$1,400" was the dashboard's default blended (`ACTIVE`+`SHADOW`) total; the real, live-visible fires that day were only 4 trades / +$150.
- Pulling daily real (`origin_status='ACTIVE'`) P&L for all 25 days since origin_status tracking began (2026-07-17) found **no large-loss-day pattern to manage** — worst real day in 5 weeks is -$485, and TREND days show the *best* real average (+$420/day), not the worst. The multi-thousand-dollar "bad days" visible in the blended total are entirely SHADOW-origin (suppressed) background noise from setup_types not in the current live roster — the SUPPRESS/THIN_N pipeline already appears to be doing the job the user was asking how to build. `RESEARCH_CLAIM real_vs_blended_daytype_pnl_20260812` (thin, N=25 days, PROVISIONAL — not a decisive claim).
- Real gap found (flagged as `OPEN_DECISION daytype_alpha_missing_for_live_roster`, MEDIUM) and **fully resolved same-session, 2026-08-12 follow-up**: the "zero rows for any of the 19 live setup_types" claim above was itself inaccurate — 10 of 19 do have real `DAY_TYPE_ALPHA` cells. The real bug was one level deeper: those cells' `SIZE_UP`/`SUPPRESS`/etc. recommendations are computed from **blended N** (can be majority `BACKFILL` synthetic data) and feed live `sizeMultiplier` directly (`acd.js`'s `dtaRow`, ~line 6791/6883-6889/6953-6965) with **no real-N floor** — the exact same bug class already fixed for `IB_BULLISH`/`IB_BEARISH` on 2026-07-28 (`REAL_N_FLOOR=5`), never extended to the other 9 live FADE-type setups that also read this path. Fixed: added `realN`/`realEv` to `liveStats._dta`, gated `dtaRow` on `REAL_N_FLOOR=5` — **direction-aware** (2-round DeepSeek design + code review): only `SIZE_UP`/`SIZE_UP_STRONG` is gated on thin real N or bad real EV; `SUPPRESS`/`SIZE_DOWN` is left alone regardless of real N, since dropping those on "unproven" grounds would remove a live protection rather than defang false upside — a direction-blind version of this fix (my own first draft) would have been a regression, caught by DeepSeek's design critique before it shipped.
